# Decisions

Append-only, as in kernel's `docs/decisions.md`: a decision changes by a new entry that supersedes it.
Status: **decided** (owner agreed), **adopted** (recommended and taken; confirm or overturn), **open**.

| ID | Decision | Status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S-01 | **Substrate is an integration, not a new core.** It assembles existing projects into one stack and adds only the glue between them. Kernel stays the world model, Morphogen stays the judge; neither is forked. Each is vendored as a git submodule at a pinned commit (kernel `c23a982`, 2026-10-19; Morphogen `4f36687`, 2026-11-05) | adopted |
| S-02 | **The claims format is kernel's.** One Postgres (kernel D-01) holds every claim: attributed, referenced, belief-scored, scoped, bitemporal (D-09), written only by change sets (D-05), exported as N-Quads (D-45). Graphiti and Cognee are not adopted: kernel already has what they would add (provenance, valid and recorded time, recall by BM25 and embeddings, D-60 and D-61), plus clearance and review, which they lack. A graph engine comes only as a projection where an algorithm pays off (D-08) | adopted |
| S-03 | **The harness is Claude Code.** It fills the cognitive cycle, the builder and the mapper's hands. It reaches kernel through `kk up` (MCP memory profile, skill, hooks; kernel D-57, D-88) and Morphogen through its plugin, installed from GitHub so the judge is never the candidate (Morphogen CLAUDE.md) | adopted |
| S-04 | **The model gateway is Bifrost**, image `maximhq/bifrost:v2.3.2` (transports v2.3.2, 2026-10-20; v2.4.0 and v2.5.0 are under two weeks old). Apache-2.0 (its `LICENSE`, read 2026-11-06). Claude Code reaches it at `/anthropic` with a virtual key, or with a claude.ai subscription plus the key in `x-bf-vk` (Bifrost's `docs/cli-agents/claude-code.mdx`). Why: every model call's tokens and cost are measured outside the agent (Morphogen issue #43) and exposed as Prometheus metrics (`bifrost_cost_total`, `bifrost_input_tokens_total`, `bifrost_output_tokens_total` in its telemetry plugin) | adopted |
| S-05 | **Telemetry goes through Prometheus into kernel.** Prometheus `v3.15.0` at the digest kernel's devops demo pins scrapes Bifrost; kernel's `observer-prometheus` records each window as observed claims (kernel D-52), so cost and errors become claims the MAPE-K loop and Morphogen can both read. Morphogen keeps its own `otel` observer for judged runs | adopted |
| S-06 | **The deployment overlays kernel's compose project.** `deploy/compose.yaml` is an overlay on `vendor/kernel/deploy/compose.yaml`, the way kernel's devops demo is, so `kk up` keeps working unchanged and the substrate adds services beside it. Licences stay kernel's posture (D-11): permissive or LGPL libraries in the default stack | adopted |
| S-07 | **Identity resolution is kernel's M6-T9, judged by Morphogen.** The resolver is a kernel module (Splink, MIT, as M6-T9 names; `skos:exactMatch` when certain, `closeMatch` for review). Morphogen's entity-resolution demo supplies its cells, held-out pairs and verdicts. One resolver, not two | adopted |
| S-08 | **Verdicts become claims.** A new module, `encoder-morphogen`, reads Morphogen's evidence log and submits each verdict as a DQV measurement with PROV lineage, under its own role. Kernel then knows what was judged, by whom, at what cost; Morphogen's log stays the integrity record | adopted |
| S-09 | **The map is kernel's, with Backstage files as a source.** `catalog-info.yaml` describes the substrate (and later, a customer's systems). Morphogen reads it as cells today; a new module, `encoder-backstage`, reads it into kernel as `kk-tosca` claims (kernel D-07). Kernel's UI is the human view; a Backstage server is optional and not in the default stack | adopted |
| S-10 | **First distribution: the system minding itself.** The first thing the stack does is keep a cited, judged model of itself: kernel's self-encoder and the Backstage encoder build the map, Bifrost and Prometheus observe, Morphogen judges changes, `encoder-morphogen` closes the loop. When kernel's distributions land (`kk up --distribution`, kernel D-84 c), substrate becomes one | adopted |
| O-1 | ~~Where the repository lives on GitHub~~ answered: `OpenReplicant/Substrate` (owner, 2026-11-06) | decided |
| O-2 | Whether Bifrost stores its logs in kernel's Postgres (a separate database) or its own SQLite volume (default here: its volume) | open |
| S-11 | **Kernel and Morphogen are full clones in `repos/`, not submodules** (supersedes S-01's vendoring; owner, 2026-11-06: "clone the repos ... but be able to contribute upstream as we test fixes"). `scripts/bootstrap.sh` clones each on `main` from `repos.yaml`, which records the commit substrate was last checked against; `repos/` is gitignored. A fix found here goes upstream by branch and PR in that repository (the `upstream` skill); `upstream/<repo>/*.patch` holds fixes prepared but not yet opened | adopted |
| S-12 | **Backstage is in the stack** (supersedes S-09's "optional"; owner, 2026-11-06). Generated with `@backstage/create-app` 0.9.3 (2026-10-20) into `services/backstage`, built by Backstage's host build into `localhost/substrate-backstage:local`, reading `catalog-info.yaml`, in-memory SQLite (the catalog is re-read from files at start). Its generated backend already includes `plugin-mcp-actions-backend`: Claude Code reaches the catalog at `/api/mcp-actions/v1` with a static token limited to `mcp-actions` and `catalog` (backstage.io/docs/ai/mcp-actions). Guest sign-in, local only | adopted |
| S-13 | **Podman by default, Docker by switch** (owner, 2026-11-06). `CONTAINER_ENGINE` in `.env` picks the engine for every script. Kernel's `kk up` calls `docker` directly, so `scripts/up.sh` starts the whole stack itself and calls `kk up --no-stack` for logins and harness wiring; the upstream fix is `upstream/kernel/0001-*.patch` (`KK_CONTAINER_ENGINE`). With Podman, `podman compose` needs the docker-compose provider (or podman-compose) | adopted |
| S-14 | **Gateway providers: Claude, OpenRouter, local** (owner, 2026-11-06). `scripts/render_bifrost.py` writes Bifrost's `config.json` from `.env`: Anthropic always (with an API key if given, else the Claude subscription passed through), OpenRouter if a key is given, Ollama on the host if enabled; one virtual key `substrate` for all of them; auth enforced on inference. The file seeds Bifrost's own config store, so dashboard edits persist. Claude Code is routed by `scripts/gateway.py on` (subscription mode by default: `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS: x-bf-vk`), applied only after the gateway answers | adopted |
| S-15 | **One `.env` for the stack** at the repository root (mode 600, gitignored), holding kernel's settings and substrate's; secrets are generated by `scripts/up.sh` the first time. Kernel's own `deploy/.env` is not used | adopted |
| O-3 | Whether to open the kernel engine fix (S-13) as a PR now, or after it is tested with Podman on the owner's machine (recommended: after) | open |
| S-16 | **The runtime is a slot** (owner, 2026-11-06: "an opinion that should be swappable"). `RUNTIME` in `.env` names a script in `scripts/runtime/` that defines four verbs, `rt_up`, `rt_down`, `rt_status`, `rt_logs`; `up.sh`, `down.sh`, `logs.sh` and `check.sh` call only those. Two runtimes now: `compose` (kernel's compose project plus `deploy/compose.yaml`) and `nomad` (`deploy/nomad/`). Both read the same `.env`, the same rendered gateway config and the same host folder for kernel's artifacts (`.state/artifacts`), so a project moves between them without re-reading. A third (k3s) is a new file in `scripts/runtime/` plus its manifests | adopted |
| S-17 | **Nomad is the control plane** (owner, 2026-11-06: one control plane across containers, processes and clusters; Nomad "already the right shape"). Chosen over k3s because one scheduler runs containers (Podman or Docker driver), plain processes (`raw_exec`) and later clusters with the same job spec, with built-in service discovery, encrypted Variables, per-task resource limits, Prometheus metrics for allocations, and an ACL'd API a kernel actuator can act through. k3s would give Apache-2.0 and the Kubernetes ecosystem, but processes need adapters and it runs containerd, not Podman. Pins: Nomad 2.0.7 (2026-09-17; 2.1.0 is under two weeks old), `nomad-driver-podman` 0.6.5 (2026-07-13, MPL-2.0; 0.6.6 is under two weeks old; built against Nomad 1.11, confirm it loads on 2.0.7), installed by `scripts/install_hashicorp.py` with SHA-256 checked against HashiCorp's published sums. Single-node agent on 127.0.0.1 (`deploy/nomad/agent.hcl`); one job, five groups (db, kernel, gateway, map, telemetry); host networking on loopback; secrets in one Variable per group with only that group's keys; Prometheus finds targets through Nomad's own service catalog. **Linux only** (the Podman driver is), so `compose` stays the default until `scripts/check.sh` passes on Nomad on the owner's machine; then a new row makes `nomad` the default | adopted |
| S-18 | **Nomad's licence is BUSL-1.1, not open source.** Its additional use grant allows production use except offering Nomad to third parties as a hosted or embedded product that competes with HashiCorp's paid versions; each version becomes MPL-2.0 four years after release (`LICENSE` at v2.0.7, read 2026-11-06). This is outside kernel's D-11 posture (permissive or LGPL in the default stack), which is why Nomad is a runtime choice and not a dependency: nothing in kernel, Morphogen or substrate's modules imports or requires it, and `compose` runs everything without it. Before substrate ships to customers with Nomad inside, the owner decides whether that use is within the grant | open |
| S-19 | **Everything is built and run in containers; the host needs only git, python3 (standard library), the engine (with compose for that runtime) and Claude Code** (owner, 2026-11-06, after bootstrap stopped on a missing gitleaks). Kernel already builds `kk`, `kernel-mcp` and gitleaks into its image; `deploy/tools/Containerfile` adds git and uv 0.13.4 (PyPI, MIT OR Apache-2.0) to make `substrate-tools`. One long-lived container runs it as the host user (`--user`, plus `--userns keep-id` under rootless Podman), on the host network, seeing only this checkout, `~/.config/knowledge-kernel` and the connected projects, each at its host path (`scripts/tools.sh`). The commands in `bin/` exec into it, including kernel's MCP server and hooks that Claude Code starts; `kk up` runs there and `scripts/point_harness.py` rewrites the commands it wrote to `bin/`. Backstage builds in two container stages (`services/backstage/Containerfile`) and hands its `yarn.lock` back. Morphogen's judged runs stay with its plugin on the host (they start sandboxes of their own). Fixes bootstrap's check, which counted "gitleaks" as "git" | adopted |
| O-4 | Upstream to kernel: a `kk up` option naming where its MCP and hook commands live (for example `--commands-dir`), so substrate no longer rewrites them after the fact | open |
| S-20 | **One front door: Caddy, with plugins by listing them** (owner, 2026-11-07: "expand the base stack a little to include Caddy (which can have plugins)"). Kernel's stack already runs Caddy 2.11.7 at 127.0.0.1:8080 (kernel pins `caddy:2.11.7-alpine`; 2.11.8 and 2.11.9 are under two weeks old). Substrate takes over that service with `deploy/caddy/Caddyfile`: kernel's routes unchanged, plus `/console/`; the same file serves both runtimes, with addresses from the environment. Plugins: one pinned Go module per line in `deploy/caddy/plugins.txt`; empty (the default) uses the stock image, otherwise `scripts/build.sh caddy` builds with xcaddy on the official `caddy:<version>-builder-alpine` image and copies the binary onto `caddy:<version>-alpine`, as the image's docs describe. Apache-2.0 | adopted |
| S-21 | **The bus is pgmq in kernel's Postgres, in a database of its own** (owner, 2026-11-07: "maybe PGmq since we're already flying on postgres?"). pgmq v1.13.1 (2026-10-19, commit `8eab182`, PostgreSQL License; v1.13.0 2026-09-07). It is plain SQL, so `deploy/postgres/Containerfile` copies its extension files onto kernel's Postgres image (checked out by tag, verified by commit) and `scripts/bus.py` runs `CREATE EXTENSION pgmq` in database `substrate`, owned by login `substrate_bus`, never in kernel's `kk`. Queues: `triggers`, `owner`, `events` (`deploy/bus/queues.txt`). The bus carries work and notices between parts; facts stay kernel claims written by change sets. Kernel's own LISTEN/NOTIFY path (D-32, D-35) is unchanged; a kernel module that bridges it to the bus would go upstream. Compose drops kernel's `build` of the db service with `!reset` (Compose 2.24+) and runs `localhost/substrate-db:local` | adopted |
| S-22 | **The console: one live page, signed in with a kernel login** (owner, 2026-11-07: "push it with some kind of live/polled data and a simple text user/pass"). `services/console`: a small Starlette app run in kernel's image (no new dependencies; HTTP through the standard library, since kernel's image has `httpx2`, not `httpx`) behind the front door at `/console/`. The name and password are the person's kernel login (`kk person add`; kernel's `/ui/login` checks them with Postgres and returns an 8-hour token), kept in an HttpOnly, SameSite=Strict cookie; every request is checked with kernel's `whoami`, and kernel decides what the person may see and review (invariant 4). No second password store. It polls every 10 s: service health, spend from Prometheus, kernel's review queue (approve or decline through kernel's `review` tool), Morphogen verdicts (its evidence log, read-only), bus depths, the clones' refs, the spec's build plan. Proposing a goal puts a message on the `owner` queue. Alpine.js is kernel's pinned copy (3.17.4), served from the same origin under a strict CSP | adopted |
| S-23 | **The spec lives in the repository and builds itself out while it is there** (owner, 2026-11-07: "add the Substrate spec in such a way that the first run can build it out if left in place"). `docs/spec.md` (the copy sessions follow; the Claude Doc is for discussion) and `docs/build.yaml` (its ten steps, each with status, dependencies, whether it needs the owner, and the check that proves it). A SessionStart hook committed in `.claude/settings.json` (`scripts/spec.py hook`, standard library) names the next step; the `build-out` skill works one step per session, setup first, and stops at the owner's decisions. Deleting `docs/spec.md` stops it | adopted |
| S-24 | **One Postgres, kernel's image, with every extension; pgGraph is the graph engine** (owner, 2026-11-07: "build kernel's image with all the PG-plugins so there's only one Postgres instance"; pgGraph "already settled"). Supersedes S-21's substrate-built image: kernel's `deploy/postgres/Dockerfile` now carries pgvector, pg_cron, pgmq 1.13.1 and pgGraph 1.2.1 (kernel D-94, PR OpenReplicant/kernel#103), so substrate builds and runs kernel's image in both runtimes and only creates what it uses (pgmq in database `substrate`). pgGraph (github.com/evokoa/pggraph, Apache-2.0, 2026-09-22) gives graph queries over tables, `kernel.current_edge` first, enforcing row-level security as the calling role; it replaces Apache AGE, so no graph engine is open | decided |
| S-25 | **Forges are optional and interchangeable; approvals are not pull requests** (owner, 2026-11-07: a forge "should be accessible as github-alternatives where it would be used ... just an optional thing developers might want"). No forge joins the stack. Wherever substrate clones or proposes a change upstream, any git forge works: `repos.local.yaml` (gitignored) points a clone at a Forgejo, Gitea or GitLab mirror or fork, and the `upstream` skill proposes the change in that forge's way. Approval stays where it is: kernel's review lane for claims, Morphogen verdicts for changes meant to improve something, the owner for decisions that are theirs | decided |
| S-26 | **O-3 and O-4 answered: both upstream** (owner, 2026-11-07: "yes", to opening them while kernel is developed alongside substrate). O-3: the `KK_CONTAINER_ENGINE` fix is kernel#105 (kernel D-96), opened before the Podman test; the test happens with substrate's setup on the owner's machine. O-4: `kk up --commands-dir` is kernel#104 (kernel D-95); `scripts/up.sh` passes `bin/`, and `scripts/point_harness.py` stays only to repair projects connected by an older kernel | decided |
| S-27 | **Nomad stays the control plane; Kubernetes and others are optional add-ons** (owner, 2026-11-07: "if nomad will suffice and we are already geared up for it ... kubernetes or anything else would be still an optional add-on"). Runtime reconciliation (a job spec against what runs) is Nomad's, not rebuilt: `nomad job plan` and the allocations API are its evidence, and catalog components carry the Backstage Nomad plugin's annotations (`nomad.io/job-id`, `nomad.io/group`; @backstage-community/plugin-nomad, Apache-2.0) so a Backstage install can show their allocations. What substrate adds is narrower: comparing the declared map with observed calls and behaviour, and deciding which way a gap closes (spec 1, "The self-model has two faces") | decided |
| S-28 | **OpenTelemetry everywhere it can go, into one collector** (owner, 2026-11-07: "definitely add otel collectors wherever"). `otel/opentelemetry-collector-contrib:0.163.2` (2026-10-21; 0.164.0 and 0.165.0 are under two weeks old; Apache-2.0), one config for both runtimes (`deploy/otel/collector.yaml`): OTLP in on 4317 and 4318 (published on 127.0.0.1); the `servicegraph` connector (alpha) turns traces into `traces_service_graph_request_total{client, server}`, the observed call graph, with virtual nodes for uninstrumented peers such as model providers; `spanmetrics` gives per-service rates; metrics go to Prometheus through its exporter; logs (Claude Code's events) to `.state/otel/logs.jsonl` for episodes later. Checked here with the collector binary: a console-to-kernel span pair became an edge, a gateway call with `gen_ai.system` became a virtual `anthropic` node, logs reached the file. **Senders now:** Caddy (its standard `tracing` directive, spans named front-door), Bifrost (its `otel` plugin, `genai_extension`, content never exported), Backstage (`@opentelemetry/auto-instrumentations-node` 0.82.0, 2026-10-20, Apache-2.0, loaded with NODE_OPTIONS), Claude Code (`scripts/telemetry.py on`: metrics, events and beta traces, per its monitoring docs; prompts not exported). **Not yet:** kernel's `kk serve` and the console (kernel's image has the OpenTelemetry API but not the SDK; that is a kernel change, upstream). **Names join the two faces:** `service.name` is the catalog component's name, or its `substrate.dev/otel-service` annotation (`claude-code` is `cycle`, `kernel` is `kernel-mcp`) | decided |
| S-29 | **The console's Map: intended, observed, and the difference** (owner, 2026-11-07: "render both views ... as graphs in the UI"). `/console/#map` draws the catalog with Cytoscape.js (kernel's pinned 3.34.3, MIT) in one fixed layout, a column per system, three ways: intended (built, being built, planned, from `catalog-info.yaml` and `docs/build.yaml` through `substrate.dev/step`), observed (running parts from health, Prometheus, Nomad and spans; calls in the last hour), and the difference (declared and seen, declared but not seen, seen but not declared). A gap is claimed only where its evidence could exist: calls between two parts that both send spans, a metrics source that answers, a part that should send telemetry; everything else is "not observable yet", so instrumenting a part turns its edges from grey to checked | adopted |
| S-30 | **Every part on the main path sends traces; the Map claims a gap only where a trace could show one** (owner, 2026-11-07: "yeah that sounds good"). Kernel's `kk serve` traces requests, Postgres statements and outbound calls (kernel D-97, PR OpenReplicant/kernel#106); compose and Nomad give kernel and the console the collector's address, and the console traces through kernel's module when the image carries it. Caddy makes server spans only, so its spans are named per route after the component the route serves (`console`, `kernel-mcp`), and the Map reads `front-door` edges from those names. Database calls appear as nodes named by `db.name`, joined to the catalog by `substrate.dev/otel-service` (`kk` is claims-store, `substrate` is the bus). A component lists in `substrate.dev/untraced` the relations no trace can see (Claude Code's stdio MCP and plugin calls), which the Map shows as not observable rather than as gaps. Checked against stand-ins: every declared edge on the main path confirmed, and one real gap left, the model providers behind the gateway, which the catalog does not declare | adopted |
| S-31 | **Secrets are a slot; OpenBao is the store behind it** (owner, 2026-11-07: "a secret store like a lightweight open source vault, but also a swappable point in the stack"). `SECRETS` in `.env` names a backend in `scripts/secrets/` with five verbs (`sec_start`, `sec_stop`, `sec_get`, `sec_put`, `sec_status`); `deploy/secrets.txt` lists which settings are secrets and how each is made. Every reader goes through it: `env_value` in the shell scripts, `scripts/settings.py` in Python, compose's `${...}` (exported into the compose process's environment, never written), Nomad Variables. Two backends: **env** (`.env`, mode 600; the default until checked on the owner's machine, as compose was before Nomad) and **openbao**: OpenBao 2.7.1 (2026-10-01; 2.7.2 is under two weeks old; MPL-2.0, the Linux Foundation fork of Vault), one container on 127.0.0.1:8200 started outside the runtime so secrets exist before anything else. It unseals itself with a static key and sets itself up declaratively on first start (kv-v2 at `substrate/`, a `substrate` login allowed only that mount, file audit), so no root token or recovery keys are ever handed out; storage is its integrated Raft (2.7 has no file storage). Checked here with the 2.7.1 binary: self-initialisation, restart without re-initialising, the login refused outside its mount, values absent from the audit log, `migrate` from `.env`, generation in `up.sh` (twice, idempotent). On one machine the root of trust is still a file the owner holds (`.state/openbao/keys`); what OpenBao adds is one API with policies, audit, versions, and room for per-service logins and dynamic Postgres credentials. Licence: MPL-2.0 for an unmodified service and unmodified data packages is accepted (owner, 2026-11-07, with certifi in kernel D-97) | decided |
| S-32 | **Spec step 1 is built in kernel; substrate runs it after every judged run** (owner, 2026-11-07: "ok continue"). Verdicts become claims through kernel's `encoder-morphogen` (kernel D-99, PR OpenReplicant/kernel#108), in kernel because its modules come only from its repository (kernel D-43): it checks Morphogen's hash chain itself, then records each verdict about `urn:kk:verdict:<project>:<seq>` with DQV measurements and PROV commits, in vocabulary kernel already has. Substrate's part: `scripts/verdicts.sh [PROJECT]` runs it in the tools container as the module's agent (`kk_authenticator` switching to `kkm_encoder_morphogen`), on `<git common dir>/morphogen/evidence.db`, into `.state/artifacts`; a PostToolUse hook in `.claude/settings.json` runs it, async and quiet, after any Morphogen tool call. Step 1 stays `doing` until its check passes after a real judged run on the owner's machine | decided |
| S-33 | **Spec step 2: the catalog in its own terms, the calls observed, and the gaps between them in the review lane** (owner, 2026-11-07: "Do we really need our want to map to kk-tosca? Maybe it should be left in its original form and if additional vocabulary is to be mixed in or we want sssom mapping to make sense going between ontologies, that makes more sense"). Built in kernel (D-100, PR OpenReplicant/kernel#110): a `backstage` pack keeps Backstage's catalog model in its own names, and kernel's mappings pack relates it to kk-tosca and ORG; `encoder-backstage` reads `catalog-info.yaml` into it, each claim citing its line; the metrics observer records the collector's service graph as observed `kkt:connectsTo` calls; `map-drift` compares the two through the mappings and puts each gap in the review lane, derived from the catalog's claims and the observer's, so a person decides whether the map or the system is wrong (S-27: kernel compares two sets of claims; Nomad and the collector stay the reconciler and the sensor). Substrate's part: `scripts/map.sh [PROJECT]` runs the encoder in the tools container (from `up.sh`, and from a PostToolUse hook after an edit to a `catalog-info.yaml`); the kernel container runs `kk serve` parts `observer` and `drift` in both runtimes (the parts list is in `deploy/compose.yaml`, which `scripts/runtime/nomad.sh` reads; `KK_SERVE_PARTS` in `.env` overrides it), with `PROMETHEUS_URL` and `deploy/observer/queries.yaml` (the service graph's client/server pairs, and the front door's per-route spans). The catalog gains `map-drift` (inside the kernel process) and kernel-mcp's use of `metrics-api` (the observer queries Prometheus from there). `scripts/check.sh` reports the catalog's claims, the calls observed in the last hour and the gaps awaiting review (`scripts/map_check.py`). Known: under Nomad's host networking a call to a part that sends no spans is named by its address (`127.0.0.1`) in the service graph, so it shows as an undeclared call until the caller sets `peer.service`; the review lane is where that is decided. Recall answers "what depends on Bifrost?" in two hops (kernel D-100) | decided |
| S-34 | **The gateway starts with an admin account; its /metrics stays open.** Bifrost v2.3.2 locks `/api` and `/metrics` (403) until an admin account exists, though `setting-up.mdx` says a fresh instance is open; so `scripts/render_bifrost.py` seeds `governance.auth_config` (user `admin`, password `env.BIFROST_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, a generated secret in `deploy/secrets.txt`) and `client.whitelisted_routes: ["/metrics"]` (keys from `transports/config.schema.json` at `transports/v2.3.2`, read 2026-11-08). Why: Prometheus scrapes without credentials (counts and cost, no content; the port is bound to 127.0.0.1) and the dashboard is password-protected from the first start. Also from the first bootstrap on Podman 4.9: every image is named with its registry (Podman with no `unqualified-search-registries` refuses short names), and `scripts/settings.sh` (settings and generated secrets) runs in bootstrap before any build, because compose needs the secrets even to build | decided |
| S-35 | **Telemetry's placeholder nodes are not observed calls.** The observer's service-graph query (`deploy/observer/queries.yaml`) drops `client="user"` (the servicegraph connector's name for a caller that sent no span, such as a browser), `server="unknown"` (a callee no peer attribute named) and `server=":memory:"` (an in-process SQLite, Backstage's cache). Why: on the first run every one of them reached the review lane as an undeclared call (4 of 5 gaps), and the owner approved them by accident; noise that reaches a person teaches them to approve without reading. A gap they caused retracts itself once it is outside map-drift's lookback (an hour). The console's call to Backstage's readiness is declared (`console` `dependsOn: [component:map-view]`) | decided |
| S-36 | **Small defaults, open slots** (owner, 2026-11-08: "reducing some defaults but keeping interfaces adaptable for later"). Each slot keeps its interface (runtime `rt_*`, secrets `sec_*`, engine, map view, providers) and one tested default: compose, Podman, `SECRETS=env`. The others (Nomad, Docker, OpenBao) stay, marked untested until their own check passes; a second default is added when a goal needs it. A fresh-machine test (spec step 10) is what "tested" means. Why: three two-way slots were eight paths every script had to keep working, for one owner on one machine, and the one path that ran first had four bugs no dry run caught. Supersedes the "both runtimes, both engines" testing rule of S-16 once step 11 lands (spec principle 8) | decided |
| S-37 | **Triage settles the review lane at an automation level; full by default, reversible always** (owner, 2026-11-08: "allow a toggle on how automated things are with full-auto default and reversable"). `AUTOMATION=full|assisted|manual`; a `triage` agent with its own reviewer login approves or declines with its reason at `full`, recommends at `assisted`, does not run at `manual`. Kernel already refuses self-approval (KK025) and records `decided_by`, so no kernel change. Every decision has an undo in the console. Never automated at any level: owner claims (goals, policy, authority), clearance, kernel's invariants and schema, licences, spending above budget (principle 7). | S-51 | **Who decides a change depends on what it changes; no one judges a change to the thing that judges** (owner, 2026-11-08: "yes, go with that split... there should be a scenario for when Morphogen proposes a change"). A component change meant to improve it gets a Morphogen verdict, whether Claude Code or one of Morphogen's optimizers proposed it. A map revision (which way a gap closes) is the owner's in kernel's review lane, because Morphogen reads the catalog as its own rules and refuses any candidate that edits it. A part added with no claim to improve anything passes its step's check, then the map revision. A change to the judge itself (Morphogen's code, cells, rules, red team, statistics) is never judged by Morphogen: it goes through the ratchet, where a red-team test proving the hole passes the current judge first, the fix reaches Morphogen's main by a PR the owner reviews, every earlier cheat keeps failing, and A/A checks follow a statistics change. Organization is the owner's only. Spec 7, "Who decides a change". Step 2's check now says "decided by whoever decides that kind of change". `scripts/trace.sh NAME` shows a part's chain from kernel: observed, declared, each gap, its decision. Amends S-52. The shell and core panels are Alpine.js 3.17.5 (MIT) components inside the Dockview window manager; the shell exposes Alpine to plugins, which may use anything else. The data API is PostgREST v16.5 (MIT, `docker.io/postgrest/postgrest:v16.5`): the rules live in Postgres (triggers record a claim for every write, row-level security per role, SQL functions as `/rpc/<name>`), and PostgREST publishes their OpenAPI description. Sign-in follows PostgREST's documented pattern (v16.5 `how-tos/sql-user-management.rst`, read from its repository because the docs site rate-limited): pgcrypto `crypt` for passwords, a `login` function signing a JWT with pgjwt 0.2.0 (MIT, one SQL file, vendored). Database `workbench` on kernel's Postgres server (schemas `api`, `content`, `wb`, `wb_private`, `wb_crypto`), created and migrated by `scripts/workbench.sh migrate`; a plugin's JSON Schema types become `content.<type>` tables with an `api.<type>` view (`plugins/substrate`: component, skill). A trigger records a claim per field set or changed, attributed to the JWT's name. An edit supersedes the same author's earlier claim; other authors' stay beside it. Nobody deletes or writes claims directly. Sign-in is `api.login` (pgcrypto bcrypt, a JWT signed by vendored pgjwt). PostgREST v16.5 runs as service `postgrest` with `PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS=api` and `PGRST_DB_ANON_ROLE=wb_anon`; Caddy now uses `deploy/caddy/Caddyfile` (kernel's routes plus `/api`). | S-55 | **Workbench W2: the shell** (2026-11-08; owner: "think about what it should look like and don't just mush the concepts together"; "Caddy templates for the frontend with Alpine.js and/or HTMX style flows"; "tie together a bunch of disjointed UIs with iframes and launch them from a unified UI"; "a nice general-use CMS... AI-first"). It is shaped like an IDE: a sidebar for where things live, a Dockview workspace of tabs (split, float, pop out), an inspector following the one shared selection (provenance first), a dashboard of widget cards, a command palette, and apps (other UIs in a tab when they allow framing, else in their own window). Seven extension kinds (view, widget, inspector, field, command, menu, app); the core is a plugin on them, and so is `plugins/substrate/web`. Caddy serves `index.html` as a template that finds plugins (`listFiles`, `include`); data is rendered only by Alpine over the JSON API. HTMX-style fragments are a planned view kind, with Alpine AJAX (MIT) as the candidate at W3; the owner's "x-fetch" was not found as an Alpine plugin. Libraries: `@alpinejs/csp` 3.17.5 (MIT; no eval, so a strict CSP with no inline script or style) and dockview-core 8.6.2 (MIT; ES module build, stylesheet extracted from its UMD build), vendored with licences. Caddy routes `/` to the shell, `/plugins/<id>/web/*` only, `/api` to PostgREST and `/ui`, `/mcp` to kernel. Migration 003 adds `api.decisions` and `api.decide`. Checked: 25/25 store tests; served pages, MIME types, CSP and plugin-folder isolation by curl; in Chrome, the sign-in page loads with no errors of its own, every module registers (7 views, 8 fields, 5 widgets including substrate's, its kernel app), and a wrong password gets 403. Not checked by me: the signed-in screens (screenshots would not attach to the tab, and reading the test password was refused); the owner checks them. Kernel's UI refuses framing (`frame-ancestors 'none'`), so it is a window app. Found: kernel's self-encoder fails on Compose's `!override` tag in `deploy/compose.yaml` (kernel is parked; noted for upstream) | adopted |
| S-56 | **Workbench W3: the server, extractors and live updates; pgmq for work, NOTIFY for notices** (2026-11-09; owner: "go ahead with W3... we built the Postgres server with PGmq which i think has a websocket port? (that should carry the same auth pipeline?)"). pgmq has no port: it is queues in SQL inside Postgres. So it carries durable work (queue `wb_jobs`: run this extractor), and Postgres's `LISTEN/NOTIFY` carries notices (`wb_events`: this changed). The browser cannot speak Postgres, so the Workbench server (`workbench/workbench/server.py`, compose service `workbench`, Caddy `/wb/api/*` with `flush_interval -1`) relays NOTIFY as Server-Sent Events. It checks the same JWT PostgREST checks (HS256, `exp`, a `wb_*` role), and the browser sends it with `fetch` in a header, never in a URL. Events are ids only; the browser refetches through PostgREST under its own token, so Postgres still decides visibility. Extractors: plugins' `register(wb)` adds them; a run is one transaction. They write through the `api` views as `wb_server` (an editor), and claims carry their source, quote and span (migration 004 redefines `wb.record_claims`). Unchanged sources add nothing, what is gone is retracted, and decisions no longer raised are withdrawn. Substrate's plugin 0.2.0: `substrate:catalog` (75 entities, 240 relations), `substrate:claude-code` (4 skills, 3 hooks), `substrate:drift` (one real gap: the `knowledge-kernel` skill `kk up` installs is not declared), plus a Map view; the shell gains live views, a Live indicator, and an Extractors view. Checked: `tests/test_w3.py` 19/19 and `test_w1.py` 25/25 in throwaway databases. Over HTTP through Caddy: events refuse no token or a forged one (401); a viewer may list but not run extractors (403); an editor's queued run is taken by the worker, and the viewer's open stream gets the run's start, its decision and its end. Moved to W4: Morphogen's verdicts, recall, MCP. The server runs on kernel's image (Starlette 1.8.0); `workbench/Containerfile` pins its own for when it leaves | decided |
| S-57 | **Workbench W4: kernel leaves the stack; Workbench is substrate's store, with recall, Morphogen's verdicts and MCP** (2026-11-09; owner: "go ahead with W4"). **Recall:** `/api/rpc/search` (migrations 005 to 007), full-text over every type's text fields and every claim's quote, any word as a prefix, as the caller; the palette and MCP use it; vectors wait for an embedding model. **Verdicts:** `substrate:morphogen` reads `<git common dir>/morphogen/evidence.db`, checks the whole hash chain itself, and records each judge verdict as a `verdict` item citing its record; tested against Morphogen's own writer, including a tampered log refused with nothing kept (`plugins/substrate/tests/test_morphogen.py`). No judged run exists here yet. **MCP:** `POST /wb/api/mcp` (`workbench/workbench/mcp.py`, written to the protocol's base methods, no SDK), ten tools, each forwarding the agent's token to PostgREST. Agents are person rows with roles that sign in by token: `workbench token NAME --header` for Claude Code's `headersHelper` (read in its MCP docs 2026-11-09), a fresh one-hour token per connection, none stored. Agent `claude-code` (editor). **Kernel out:** `deploy/compose.yaml` stands alone (project `substrate`: db, postgrest, workbench, caddy), on the same volume `knowledge-kernel_db-data`. Kernel's database `kk` (6399 claims) is kept in it, untouched. The Postgres image is vendored (`deploy/postgres/Dockerfile`, from kernel at d9e800b); the Workbench server and the tools container have their own images (`python:3.13.17-slim-bookworm`; gitleaks 8.30.1 checked by SHA-256). Removed: `bin/kk`, `bin/kernel-mcp*`, `scripts/map_check.py`, `point_harness.py`, `modules/` (its briefs in git history), Caddy's `/ui` and `/mcp`. `scripts/map.sh` and `verdicts.sh` keep their names (so their hooks do) and run Workbench's extractors. `scripts/harness.py` backed up and rewrote this project's Claude Code wiring (kernel's MCP entry and 4 hooks out, Workbench's MCP in). Kernel is an option in the map; spec step 3 is done by Workbench (S-52's route), and step 2 waits on the owner's decision on the `knowledge-kernel` skill gap (recommended: decline, then remove that kk-installed skill). Found in the browser: unchanged extractor runs touched `updated_at` (fixed: only changed columns are written); the palette's input missed focus when frames were slow (fixed: focus after the frame x-show reveals it). Checked: tests 25/25, 19/19, 5/5; MCP and roles over HTTP; the shell in the owner's Chrome | decided |
| S-58 | **Spec step 2 done; the review lane where it can be found; gaps decided where they are seen** (2026-11-09; owner: "the review lane was hard to find, put it under system maybe?", and yes to deciding on the Map). The loop ran once on substrate: drift raised `undeclared:skill:knowledge-kernel` (decision 16427fcb), the owner declined it (change the repository), the skill `kk up` had installed was moved to `.state/removed/`, and `substrate:claude-code` retracted its claims (84a61be0, 31013bf8); `scripts/trace.sh knowledge-kernel` shows each link by id. The review lane moves to the top of Workspace as "Review lane" with a live count of open decisions (a `badge` on menu entries, set through `wb.state.badges`), rather than System, where it would be harder to find; Claims moves to System. | S-59 | **Spec step 4 done: choices come from catalogs; the first is the graph endpoint, Ontop** (2026-11-09; owner: "do what you think is best, but keep it simple... planning for search and reasoners. maybe this is where the CMS gains super powers?"). **How a choice is made:** the `research` skill (`.claude/skills/research`): write the need first, search the `sources` registries, record candidates in `docs/choices/<slug>.yaml`, test the shortlist with a pinned trial under `scripts/trials/<slug>/`, record the pick and every rejection with its reason. `substrate:choices` (substrate's plugin 0.3.0, run by `scripts/up.sh`) reads each file into Workbench as a `choice` item and `candidate` items: licence, latest release and its date, last push, stars and archived quoted from GitHub's and deps.dev's JSON (the answer kept as a source), fit, trial and outcome quoted from the file; a registry that does not answer leaves earlier claims standing. Kernel having left (S-57), this lives in the plugin, not as a kernel module upstream (step 4's text updated to say so). **The choice:** a SPARQL endpoint over Workbench's items, claims and relations, with reasoning. Searched GitHub (topic:sparql, topic:r2rml), deps.dev and image registries; eight candidates. Tried four on the same three queries over the live tables (subclass reasoning: 85 parts expected; a claim lookup: 5; subproperty reasoning: 110) and on whether a new relation shows at once: **Ontop 5.5.0** (`docker.io/ontop/ontop:5.5.0`, Apache-2.0, released 2026-02-14) answered all and saw the new relation (it rewrites SPARQL to SQL over the live tables); Jena Fuseki 6.2.0 answered all from a copy (runner-up for a materialised graph with rule reasoners); Oxigraph 0.5.13 has no reasoning (0 and 0) but is 9.5 MB (the pick for a plain projection); RDFLib 7.6.0 with OWL-RL 7.6.2 answered all in process, not an endpoint (kept in mind for the vocabulary loader). Not shortlisted: QLever (prebuilt index for billions of triples, no reasoning), Virtuoso (GPL-2.0 only, per its LICENSE), Blazegraph (GPL-2.0, last release 2020), Morph-KGC (a materialiser; the tool for an RDF export from the same R2RML). No Morphogen verdict: the need decided it and only one candidate met it; a verdict is the test when two do. Ontop is an option in the map (`graph-endpoint`), not running: it waits for content types to carry terms (x-term) so its mapping can be generated, and needs a read-only database role. Costs recorded: about 430 MB of JVM, OWL 2 QL reasoning only (no transitive properties or rules). |
| S-60 | **The plan after step 4: a scenario interview for step 5; vocabularies, a graph view and enforced slots as steps 7 to 9** (2026-11-09; owner: "make whatever updates you'd like to for the docs and the plan (i like everything so far...)", after asking about OWL-RL "as an optional learning/inference structure over any data gathered", a catalog of ontologies, running Ontop and pgGraph over the same data, and whether substrate is pluggable "in the sense that it has a map of component slots with interfaces that anything could fit in if it passes the tests"; on step 5: "i don't have access to any but we could try a demo interview with a decision maker trying the software for their business"). **Step 5** is rewritten: the owner, a real person, answers as a decision maker trying substrate for a business, each statement a claim marked as a scenario; a real open-source system, picked through the research skill, stands for that business's existing system and is mined as the observed half; questions are drawn from the gaps between the two. **Step 7, vocabularies first-class:** `x-term` on content types and fields, a loader for SKOS, OWL, Turtle and SSSOM, ontology registries as source catalogs, vocabularies chosen through the research skill (candidates PROV-O, SPDX, DOAP, SKOS). **Step 8, a graph view:** views over the same tables and never copies: pgGraph for traversal (as the caller), Ontop for SPARQL with reasoning (read-only role, behind Caddy), both mappings generated from the terms; OWL 2 RL inferences written back as claims attributed to the reasoner, quoting the rule and premises, retracted with them. Learning rules (induction, judged by Morphogen on held-out claims) is a held idea until there is data to hold out. **Step 9, slots enforced:** a contract test per core interface, named on the map, so an option fills a slot only by passing it; the judge stays plugged in but never a slot. | S-76 | **Spec step 17 done: streams, handlers ranked by measured cost, and the record of every call** (2026-11-10; owner: "sure", to starting step 17). **Where:** a Workbench plugin of its own, `plugins/handlers` (built to leave with Workbench; a test checks that nothing in it names substrate), with types stream, input, handler, route and call, and `ladder.py`, the contract: a handler returns Answer (with a confidence in [0, 1]), Escalate (with a reason, optionally where to), Waiting or Dropped; the runner refuses, and records as refused, an answer with no confidence, an escalation with no reason, a crash, an unregistered handler, or a handler called around the runner (`ask` records a nested call), and the input goes on up; only a person's handler sees the outcome. Substrate's part is `streams.yaml`, `scripts/streams.sh` (from up.sh, observe.sh and map.sh) and `scripts/calls.py`, through which the three hooks record their own runs (`.state/calls/hooks.jsonl`: which hook, when, how long, exit code; no input content). **Defaults:** rank orders every handler by mean measured cost per call, a person's attention first, then money, then seconds, so any person is dearer than any model and any paid call dearer than any free one; an unmeasured handler takes its kind's prior (rule 0.01 s, script and hook 1 s, model 5 s and $0.001, session 60 s and $0.10, person 1 person and an hour) until it is measured. A person's cost is one person plus the time from the input arriving to its decision, from Workbench's timestamps, never reported by the handler. Drift's gaps are an **advisory** stream: the map is the owner's (S-51), so a lower rank's answer is a recommendation scored against the decision, and every gap still reaches the review lane. The first rule is **precedent**: what a person decided before on gaps of the same kind, using only decisions made before the input arrived, with confidence agreeing ÷ (precedents + 1). **Workbench (generic):** `ctx.as_author(name)` writes values as another author under a run of its own, within the extractor's, because `wb.retract_unstated` took values written as another author under the extractor's own run for statements no longer made, and retracted 255 handler claims on the first run here; they were restored to current under such runs (a repair of derived data, each run's inputs saying so). A gap key raised twice overwrote its first input, so inputs are named by key and decision id; the 13 inputs and 26 calls named by key alone were retired, with that note. **Result here:** 14 gaps; precedent answered 4 (none scorable, all withdrawn; one recommended removing the live database's volume, which shows why the stream stays advisory) and escalated 10; the review lane answered 7 and dropped 7. Measured, the rule is cheaper than the hooks, so the ladder runs rule, hooks, review lane; the hooks stay on their prior until they next run | adopted |
| S-77 | **Spec step 18, its gateway half: Bifrost in core, model handlers metered by it; Morphogen's half is step 22, after its phase 7** (2026-11-11; owner: "merge and push, key's in .env, start step 18 ...but right now what i'm thinking about ... is Morphogen", then, on the proposal to plan Morphogen's phase 7 and do the gateway now, "write the plan as phase 7, and go ahead with the gateway"). **Morphogen:** PLAN.md phase 7 (one request in and one verdict out; judging a system where it runs; model calls counted through a gateway, #43; the core with the rest as adapters; the three-arm test; ready to show) is Morphogen PR #75, a plan only, for the owner's review; its suite passes (193 tests). **The gateway** (`docs/choices/model-gateway.yaml`): Bifrost `docker.io/maximhq/bifrost:v2.3.2` (Apache-2.0; v2.4.0 and later under two weeks old) over LiteLLM `ghcr.io/berriai/litellm-database:v1.106.4`, on a trial of ten cases run twice (`scripts/trials/model-gateway/run.py`: a fake model with fixed usage, one real free OpenRouter call, keys per caller, refusals, a rate limit, its own record, no content kept, footprint): Bifrost passed all; LiteLLM kept four rows for five answered calls in both runs and used 633 MB to Bifrost's 142. Portkey's gateway, OmniRoute, Plano and Helicone's gateway were rejected unshortlisted (reasons in the choice). Service `gateway` in `deploy/compose.yaml` (127.0.0.1:`GATEWAY_PORT`, 8090), config rendered by `scripts/gateway.py render` from `.env` and `streams.yaml` (OpenRouter when its key is set, free models only unless `GATEWAY_OPENROUTER_MODELS`; Ollama when `OLLAMA_URL` is set; a virtual key per model handler, derived from `GATEWAY_KEY_SECRET`, `GATEWAY_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR` each, 120 by default; content logging off), its config and log in a database of its own, `gateway`, on the stack's Postgres (`scripts/gateway.py init`: login `gateway` owns it, `gateway_meter` may only read), its app directory a tmpfs. v2.3.2 refuses providers on private addresses unless `allow_private_network` is set on that provider (set for Ollama). Contract `model-api` (10 checks; `scripts/contract.sh model-api`): no key or a key it did not issue refused, a paid model refused, a free one answers (or its provider's own limit comes back as such), each call kept with its caller and call id and without content, the record read-only to the meter. **Model handlers** (the handlers plugin, generic): kind `model`, entry `model`, settings in the streams file; each call carries a call id; after the run the meter reads the gateway's row and that is the call's cost (money, seconds, tokens), never the handler's timing; a call recorded under another caller's key is refused, and one the gateway never recorded is refused after `meter_grace_minutes` (15); a call the gateway turned away is made again on the next run; a handler that joins a stream later is asked on inputs already seen where its answer can count. Tests: `plugins/handlers/tests/test_models.py` (16 checks, a fake gateway with its own log). **Models** (`docs/choices/models.yaml`): picked by the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index and the Arena text leaderboard among the 13 free OpenRouter models, then changed by what answered: Inkling 2 Small refuses calls from outside agentic harnesses (403) and Gemma 4 26B A4B was rate-limited upstream, so small is `google/gemma-4-31b-it:free` (Arena 1452) and large is `nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl:free` (AA index 14); both on drift's gaps, which stays advisory. Gemma's free tier at Google AI Studio still turns most of a burst away (429), and those calls are retried. **First live results:** 30 model calls, every one metered by the gateway ($0; the large model 5 to 28 s and 395 to 1,477 tokens a call); the large model answered 5 of 15 gaps (2 scorable: 1 right, 1 wrong), was not sure on 5, and 4 of its replies were JSON with an unquoted reason, read leniently since. **Split:** counting model calls inside Morphogen's judged runs, with its red-team test, is step 22, waiting on PR #75 and phase 7c; step 18's check no longer names it. The owner's still: which models, and a monthly ceiling (the OpenRouter key's own $0.01 cap holds now). | adopted |
| S-78 | **The models stand, and which is better is ours to measure; Morphogen's phase 7 is its plan** (2026-11-11; owner: "i merged PR#75, yes the gemma & nemotron models are great picks and i think the nemotron model is more-capable but now we can distill that answer from our own task results"). The small (`google/gemma-4-31b-it:free`) and large (`nvidia/nemotron-3.5-super-vl:free`) handlers of S-77 are confirmed. The owner expects Nemotron to be the more capable; that is a hypothesis the ladder tests: each handler's answers are scored against the person's decisions (`scored`, `correct` on each handler), and its rank comes from measured cost, so which one to trust at which rank comes from substrate's own results, not from the leaderboards. Morphogen's PLAN.md phase 7 is on its main (#75, 865d4ea); `repos.yaml` is checked at that commit; step 22 now waits on phase 7c being built. | adopted |
| S-79 | **A model handler's model and prompt are searched together (step 19); Morphogen's phase 7 comes first** (2026-11-11; owner: "judging models is very task-specific and prompt-specific ... i wonder if anyone/we are optimizing model selection or the cross-section between the two", then "if the game player needs Morphogen to perform optimally maybe we should start there, and keep its uses/integrations in mind. fold your rec into step 19 ... go!"). What was found (2026-11-11): LLMSelector (Chen et al., arXiv 2502.14815, ICML 2025) picks a model per module of a compound system, without prompts; routers (Routoo arXiv 2401.13979, IPR arXiv 2509.06274, cost-aware contrastive routing arXiv 2508.12491) pick per query; Not Diamond's docs tune a prompt per model with DSPy, then train a router on the pairs' scores, in sequence; DSPy's tuned prompts are tied to the model they were tuned on; no joint search behind a judge the search cannot reach was found. **In step 19:** a model handler's prompt becomes a file of its own (today it is written in `handlers_plugin.py`); a cell names the model (choices) and the prompt (text) as what may change; Morphogen searches the pairs, the model outside and the prompt tuned for each model inside (Optuna's choices around GEPA, both in its catalog), on search seeds, and the judge passes or refuses the best pair on held-out seeds, by score with cost and seconds from the gateway, with an A/A check. Step 19 now waits on step 22, and so on Morphogen's phase 7c. **Next:** Morphogen's phase 7, from 7a, with the game player's needs in mind (held-out seeds, scored outputs, model calls counted by the gateway, a caller's services). | adopted |
| S-80 | **Streams that act are environments; ARC-AGI-1 first, then ARC-AGI-3; a ladder view and transcripts; the assistant framed, privacy rules first** (2026-11-11; owner: "Yes on all 3" (ARC-AGI-1 then ARC-AGI-3; the ladder view and per-stream transcripts in step 19; frame the assistant), "The multi-process stream fusion cognitive architecture has a decent demo and use case with the assistant, but it might also be a good framing for the game player ... we might start framing it like virtual robotics"). **Environments** (spec section 10): a stream that is a loop over time is an environment, with the interface robotics and reinforcement learning already use, `reset()` and `step(action) -> observation, reward, terminated, truncated, info`, as Gymnasium states it (Farama-Foundation/Gymnasium, MIT, v1.4.0 of 2026-10-05, read 2026-11-11); ARC-AGI-3's toolkit (arcprize/arc-agi, MIT) already offers `make`, `reset`, `step`, an action space, a scorecard and a renderer hook per frame. An episode is an input whose calls are its steps; actions pass a gate the stream declares (a game's are free; a person's world gets proposals); several streams can feed one decision, and they meet in the input a handler is shown. **Step 19** is now the environment slot with a contract test and ARC-AGI-1 as its first filler (one step per task; Apache-2.0, fchollet/ARC-AGI; evaluation tasks held out and never read while building), with transcripts kept for this stream and Workbench's ladder view; ARC-AGI-2 is later, since free models score near zero on it. **Step 23** (new, after 19): ARC-AGI-3 through its toolkit, episodes step by step, replayed frame by frame; its API key is the owner's. **The assistant** (step 21): `docs/assistant/BRIEF.md` (the calendar as the first environment, its actions proposals the owner accepts, edits or ignores; read from this machine, an ICS export or CalDAV, not a cloud connector) and `docs/assistant/PRIVACY.md` (ten rules, each with the check to build before any personal stream is read: named sources only, content stays here, only local models see it, Claude Code does not read the personal domain by default, gitleaks first, transcripts off, bounded retention, proposals not actions, stop and forget, held-out weeks). | adopted |
| S-81 | **What to change next is a stream; transfer between streams is judged on the receiving stream** (2026-11-11; owner, on "thinking about transfer learning/capability through the blackboard/DB and components" and whether "a substrate agent will explore what changes it extensions to itself ... a pretty boundless space", then "sounds good!"). Spec section 10 and step 20: the space of self-changes is never listed; each proposal cites its source (a gap, an escalation, a failing check, a cost hot spot, a catalog release, the owner); generators of proposals are handlers on a stream of their own, ranked by cost per change that lands, and the idle tier's budget follows those ranks, with a fixed small share for an archive of judged variants (stepping stones, after the Darwin Gödel Machine, arXiv 2505.22954). Transfer has three paths (a compiled-down component joins another stream's ladder; Workbench's knowledge shown in a handler's input; Morphogen's selector primed by earlier verdicts), each judged on the receiving stream's held-out cases. Two limits hold whatever is judged: held-out cases never become another stream's knowledge, and nothing leaves a person's domain. | adopted |
| S-82 | **Recall by meaning and a catalog of models by task (step 24); papers as a stream (step 25)** (2026-11-11; owner: "shouldn't we be involving vector-encoding models/search? (and cataloging models by task, etc)", and "sounds good" to papers as a stream). Step 24: an embedding model picked from MTEB among models that run here (through Ollama, behind the gateway), one vector per item field and claim quote in pgvector, named by its model; recall joins full-text (S-57) and vectors, and must beat full-text alone on held-out queries from substrate's own history; models catalogued by Hugging Face's task tags, each with the leaderboard scores it was picked on and what substrate measured per stream. Step 25, after steps 20 and 24: a query over a paper registry as a stream (published and backlog); claims and evidence as stated claims quoting the paper; code as catalog candidates; paper mining as a generator of proposals, ranked by what lands. Step 24 needs no judged run, so it can be built while Morphogen's phase 7 goes on. | adopted |
| S-83 | **Spec step 24: recall is words, meaning and the graph in one query; Qwen3-Embedding 0.6B in a local Ollama; models catalogued by task** (2026-11-11; owner: "full-text and vectors in one ranking/query, yes, but i think we can also include graph traversals to the combined query space. you can run embedding models locally on my CPU or GPU (if it doesn't compete for cycles with another GPU-model) ... Ollama container, everything looks good"). **Ollama** `docker.io/ollama/ollama:0.40.12` (MIT; 0.40.13 was 14 days old) as service `ollama` (volume `substrate_ollama-models`, 127.0.0.1:`OLLAMA_PORT` for pulling only), reached by every caller through the gateway (`OLLAMA_URL=http://ollama:11434`); on the CPU, because Podman 4.9.3 cannot parse this machine's CDI spec (`/var/run/cdi/nvidia.yaml`, cdiVersion 0.7.0: "unknown field additionalGids"), so the GTX 1070 is not passed in; one Ollama serves the embedding model and later a person's models, so they share the device through one scheduler. **The embedding model** (`docs/choices/embedding-model.yaml`): shortlisted from model cards' MTEB figures (the leaderboard page returned no table), tried on 79 queries from substrate's own history (decision rows' quotes and the parts they name; commit subjects and the parts whose files changed), 39 held out: Qwen3-Embedding 0.6B took recall at 10 from 0.067 (words) to 0.193 with vectors (10 better, 0 worse, sign p 0.002) and 0.286 with the graph to depth 2 (8 better, 0 worse, p 0.008), with an A/A pair at 0; nomic-embed-text v1.5 reached 0.143 (p 0.016) and gained nothing from the graph; Qwen3 4B was no better on the first 24 queries and 16 times slower to embed; EmbeddingGemma was rejected on its licence (Gemma terms). Not a Morphogen verdict: the trial applies Morphogen's rule itself until Morphogen can run substrate's tests (phase 7b). **Workbench** (generic): `sql/013_recall.sql` (`api.recall`: reciprocal-rank fusion of words, vectors and items up to `depth` relations from the ten best, read as the caller; `wb.embedding.digest`), `workbench/embed.py` (`workbench embed`, `workbench recall`), MCP `search` is recall; `tests/test_recall.py` (12 checks). **Substrate:** `scripts/recall.sh` (pulls the model, embeds what changed; run by up.sh, map.sh and observe.sh); gateway keys: `workbench:embed` from `.env` (`WORKBENCH_EMBED_KEY`), local providers only and uncapped, and `contract:model-api`, so the contract test never spends a handler's budget; contract `embedding-api` (8 checks). **Fixed on the way:** every capped key pointed at one rate limit, which Bifrost counts once, so all handlers shared 120 calls an hour; each caller now has its own, and `model-api` checks that no two share one. **Models by task:** candidates carry `tasks` (Hugging Face's task tags), `model` (the gateway's name), `scores` (the leaderboard figures they were picked on) and `measured` (from each handler that calls the model: calls, answers, scored, correct, mean cost, by stream), so `api/candidate?tasks=cs.["feature-extraction"]` is the catalog for a task. | adopted |
| S-84 | **The GTX 1070 for the local model server, without CDI** (2026-11-11; owner: "Ok to change that config for podman"). Podman 4.9.3 has no per-user CDI directory (`cdi_spec_dirs` is not in its containers.conf), so a readable spec would have needed root in `/etc/cdi`. Instead `deploy/compose.nvidia.yaml` passes Ollama the four device nodes (`/dev/nvidia0`, `nvidiactl`, `nvidia-uvm`, `nvidia-uvm-tools`, mode 666 on this machine) and binds the driver's libraries by their stable names (`libcuda.so.1`, `libnvidia-ml.so.1`, `libnvidia-ptxjitcompiler.so.1` from `NVIDIA_LIB_DIR`, default `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu`), so a driver upgrade needs no change; Ollama's image carries the CUDA runtime (its cuda_v12 runner takes compute 6.1; cuda_v13 skips it). `scripts/lib.sh`'s compose adds `deploy/compose.$OLLAMA_GPU.yaml` when `.env` sets `OLLAMA_GPU` (empty: the CPU), so a machine without a GPU, or with Docker's own GPU support, is unchanged. Nothing outside the repository was changed. Measured: all 488 items embedded in 21 s, model load included (on the CPU, 422 took about a minute); the model sits 100% in GPU memory (1.2 GB) for `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` (10 minutes) after a call, then frees it. `scripts/check.sh` notes where local models compute. | adopted |
| S-86 | **Spec step 10: a business vocabulary, adopted; the business plugin** (2026-11-11; owner, S-73: "business ontologies sound good... i was concerned we were inventing ontologies when we could be adopting"). **The choice** (`docs/choices/business-vocabulary.yaml`, ten candidates): no one model covers an organisation's twelve parts, so each takes its class from the model that defines it best and the rest are mapped. W3C ORG (Recommendation 2014-01-16, `org.ttl` 0.8, PDDL 1.0) for organisation, unit, role, with FOAF 0.99 (CC BY 1.0) for person and document, the classes ORG builds on; BPMN 2.0.2 (its XML namespace's metamodel names) for process and activity; OCEL 2.0 (CC BY 4.0; it publishes no IRIs, so `ocel:` names its metamodel's concepts) for event; DMN 1.5 (namespace `https://www.omg.org/spec/DMN/20230324/MODEL/`) for decision; Valueflows 1.0.0 (REA in OWL, CC BY-SA 4.0) for resource; schema.org V30.1 (CC BY-SA 3.0) `StatisticalVariable` for measure; the BMMOntology OWL of OMG BMM 1.3 (MIT, `bmm.owl` at 7e00ec38, a placeholder namespace, unchanged since 2017) for goal, since none of the named candidates has one. Rejected: REA as ISO/IEC 15944-4 (UML in a standard sold per copy; taken through Valueflows instead) and BBO (no published OWL file, namespace or licence found). **The business plugin** (`plugins/business`, Apache-2.0, generic, loaded by up.sh): the twelve types with `x-term`, `vocabulary.yaml` (the classes and 16 relations), `mappings.sssom.tsv` (35 SSSOM rows to the models not picked); extractors `business:map` (a business's map file: its domain's items and relations, an import speaking only for its own domain) and `business:bpmn` (BPMN 2.0 XML: process, flow nodes as activities, lanes as the map's roles, data objects as documents, the diagram kept); `export-map` and `export-bpmn` write them back (a process mapped without a diagram is laid out); `scripts/business.sh import|export-map|export-bpmn|demo`. A made-up demo business (Harbour Bakery: `demo/harbour-bakery.yaml`, `demo/wholesale-order.bpmn`) is in Workbench as domain `harbour-bakery`. **Workbench** (generic): a plugin's `mappings:` (SSSOM TSV) load into `wb.mapping` with IRIs expanded (`vocab.load_mappings`, migration 014: `plugin`, labels, comment). **Substrate:** the choices extractor takes a candidate's licence from the file when it has no registry to ask. **Checked:** `plugins/business/tests/test_business.py` 30/30 (terms from picked models, none minted; every mapping loaded; the map exports to the same map, re-imports changing nothing, and makes the same entities and file in an empty Workbench; another domain's import leaves it alone; the BPMN round trip keeps nodes, flows, lanes, data, defaults, event definitions and the diagram); `scripts/trials/bpmn-opens` (Node 24.22.1, bpmn-moddle 10.3.1, MIT, the parser bpmn.io's modeler reads with): the demo file, its export and the mapped invoicing process's export open with no warnings and every element drawn (24/24, 24/24, 7/7), and a planted dangling reference is caught. **Fixed on the way:** the live database had recorded an older hash for `013_recall.sql` (re-applied by hand in step 24), so `migrate` refused to go on; 013 is idempotent, so it was re-applied and its hash recorded. | adopted |
| S-87 | **Spec step 22: substrate's first judged change, its model calls counted at the gateway** (2026-11-11; owner: "ok" to 7c then step 22; "Split them" for the interface tests; "openrouter free has no hourly limit, just a 20/minute limit & 1000/day"). **Morphogen** checked at #80 (7b: env values and services a run is given; 7c: the operator's gateway joined to each run's network and a meter that counts the run's calls outside it). **The meter** (`scripts/gateway.py judge-meter open|close RUN_ID`, run in the tools container): the run gets one key, `MORPHOGEN_JUDGE_KEY` (generated, local models only, so no cap and no provider quota); close counts every call made with it from the run's start up to a marker call of the meter's own (the gateway writes its record a few seconds late). **A local model rank** (`docs/choices/local-model.yaml`): Granite 4.0 Micro (`ollama/granite4:3b`, Apache-2.0) on the GTX 1070, picked on its card's IFEval and BFCL and a trial on the 17 withdrawn gaps (17 of 17 readable, 0.98 s, 335 tokens a call); Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 as readable and slower; Qwen3.5-4B thinks by default and answered none through the gateway; `streams.yaml` gains handler `local-model`. **The cell** (`morphogen.yaml`, `component:model-handler`, in the map): only `plugins/handlers/handlers_plugin.py` may change; `bench/run.py` runs one model handler over cases in `MORPHOGEN_INPUTS`, `bench/score.py` (the base's) scores balanced accuracy against `MORPHOGEN_PRIVATE` (a constant answer scores 0.5); better is `metered.tokens` lower by 5% with balanced accuracy not falling. `scripts/judged.py drift-gaps` writes the 7 decided gaps outside the repository, every history emptied (a held-out decision sits in the history of every later case); `scripts/judged.py env` the run's database password. The handlers' `Gateway` takes one ready key, so a judged run never holds the secret that derives every caller's. **Interface tests split** (owner's pick): `scripts/contract.sh` checks the running stack and inside a sandbox fails for base and candidate alike, so each API names it as `substrate.dev/contract`; `morphogen.dev/tests` is kept for tests of the tree (model-api: the handlers' tests against a fake gateway, in a fresh `substrate-db` served per run); 15 interfaces now show as untested. **The verdict** (Morphogen evidence 3): the candidate sent the input as compact JSON; tokens fell 5.9% (2387 to 2246, 7 of 7 runs, p 0.008), balanced accuracy fell 0.25 to 0.17 (7 of 7), so it was refused. The base handler is weak (0.25, below a constant answer). **Fixed on the way:** Bifrost kept each key's old limit as a model rule after config.json dropped it, which refused the first full judged run's 121st call; `gateway.py prune` (up.sh) drops what config.json no longer has; per-caller hourly caps are off by default (`GATEWAY_REQUESTS_PER_HOUR` empty), since OpenRouter limits the account itself. The judge's earlier derived key was printed in a session; it is refused now. | adopted |
| S-88 | **Architectures are intended maps, compared on roles; papers feed them (spec section 10, steps 26 and 27)** (2026-11-12; owner: "use the system mapping function to draw up hypothetical blueprints of various cognitive architectures we could implement using substrate (either as self with its core or as a separate system it manages)", an auto-research agent that "watches streams for new papers claiming improvements in agent architecture ... builds a blueprint and componentizes the paper to test against a baseline & competing approaches ... then can integrate where proven advantages are called for", and "i don't think i'd want to break from treating everything as hypothesis/claim"). The owner's "blueprint" is an intended map. An agent architecture is a domain whose map is intended only, with one role claim (on this stream and outcome, it beats the baseline at equal cost); a role is a stream, its outcome and a held-out budget. Architectures run as separate systems; a component reaches substrate's own core only as an ordinary judged change. Comparisons are at equal metered budgets (step 22), screened by contract tests and a development set before a judged run. **The shortest path:** step 26 needs only the first half of step 19 (the environment slot, ARC-AGI-1, the ladder on it) and step 22 (done); its first comparison is two-way, which Morphogen judges as it is: the running ladder against a Soar-shaped variant (typed escalations, compile-downs from the call trace), the cheapest variant to build; the Common Model of Cognition design at `a00fb61` comes third. The idle tier (20) and papers (25, 27) make it continual. A paper's numbers stay the authors' claims; substrate's run records whether they replicated here. Open, the owner's: the held-out budget rule (fresh cases where a stream makes them, a reusable holdout where it cannot), since it changes how the judge holds out cases; it goes to Morphogen upstream first. | proposed |
| S-89 | **Privacy is a flow policy over labels; air-gapped is one setting; the session rank is a slot (spec section 7, steps 28 and 29)** (2026-11-12; owner: "privacy needs to be adjustable, it simply isn't possible in some aspects, but what might be interesting is if we can label certain data/claims as 'private' or 'secret' to block them from exfiltration under certain privacy settings", and "i would love a fully-airgapped cognitive robot that can extend its own capabilities but we may be at least a year out"). Items and claims carry labels (public, private, secret), each a claim; the owner's flow policy says where each label's content may go, changed only in the review lane; labels travel to derived claims through the premises and inputs already recorded; lowering one is a decision. Enforced at the gateway (keys by label), Workbench's read roles (Claude Code's clearance), the export and commits, and volunteered compute, each with a contract test. `docs/assistant/PRIVACY.md` becomes the default policy for private; loosening a rule is recorded. Air-gapped is every label local-only; the obstacle is the actor (Claude Code calls Anthropic's API), so the top rank becomes a slot a local agent harness is judged against. Step 21 now waits on step 28. A policy, not a guarantee (spec section 11). | proposed |
| S-90 | **A realtime assistant with ears, eyes and a face, from a phone (spec section 10, step 30)** (2026-11-12; owner: "a realtime interactive avatar assistant with eyes/ears from a mobile page/app"). A conversation is an environment whose turns climb the ladder under a time limit, reflexes cheapest; a mobile page over HTTPS on a private network (a browser allows the microphone and camera only on a secure page); the avatar drawn on the phone from its speech; models as the flow policy allows (S-89). Candidates to search through section 5: Pipecat, LiveKit Agents, Whisper-family speech-to-text, Piper or Kokoro text-to-speech; a private network and a voice framework are options (section 8). The GTX 1070's 8 GB is tight for all of it at once. Needs the owner (it reads a person's streams). | proposed |
| S-91 | **Compute is a slot; volunteered compute fills it under stricter rules for negatives (spec section 10, step 31)** (2026-11-12; owner: "involve 'untrusted compute' or 'citizen scientists' where we can employ volunteered compute to explore novel permutations and reproduce results, which may also require reproducing failed results to minimize false-negatives/sabotage"). A volunteer's result is a claim, never a verdict; verdicts stay on trusted compute and a proposer never runs their own candidate; only public work goes out (S-89); jobs are deterministic (pinned image, fixed seed, local weights by hash), redundant across volunteers unknown to each other, with canary jobs and a reliability learned per volunteer. Sabotage is lopsided (a faked loss kills a good idea unseen), so a negative is final only on a quorum of independent negatives or a trusted re-run, and a refuted candidate keeps its negatives' sources to reopen. Prior art: BOINC. | proposed |
| S-92 | **The map is an actor network; control planes, plural, run it; Workbench's plugins become workers (spec section 4, steps 32 and 33)** (2026-11-12; owner: "i had initially worked on a custom actor framework that could do it all, before realizing i was wasting my time and had 2 practical approaches if i wanted the kind of distributed actor model i was building: use kubernetes/nomad, or build on the BEAMVM if the advantages (lowest-overhead killable processes, or whatever) it provides over the former are valued in the scope of the design. so that's why Substrate describes a map of components it can control or access - essentially an 'actor network' as defined by a map, which multiple control planes can orchestrate with comms routing at least at the DNS & discovery levels, not enforcing a protocol"). No actor framework of substrate's own and no second message plane beside Postgres: a component is an actor (an address, work in, stop and restart, a supervisor) and a control plane supervises it. Each component names its control plane, and each control plane fills `runtime-api` for its components; Kubernetes and a BEAM node join Nomad as options. Routing is by the map's names through DNS and discovery; the protocol between two components is theirs, recorded on the map. The record holds without a protocol: changes and decisions are claims, model calls are metered at the gateway, connections are observed at the edges and compared with the declared ones. Within compose, each Workbench plugin runs as a worker with its own pgmq queue, its own database role and a NOTIFY wake-up (step 32); fast streams keep state in memory and write claims per turn. | proposed |
| S-93 | **Workbench is an actor framework of its own kind; the map tells controlled components from reached ones; step 33 dropped** (2026-11-12; owner: "step 33 may be unnecessary, and it might not be accurate to call all components 'actors' since things which aren't under the control plane's control can't be started, stopped, or restarted - those would be remote services, but still observable as part of a system/network", and "Substrate or Workbench (whichever it should fall under) may actually be an actor framework or something LIKE one, worth being explicit about, and designing around like plugins as workers with independent queues and permissions"). Supersedes S-92 where they differ. Not every component is an actor: **controlled** components run under a control plane substrate drives (start, stop, restart, change); **reached** ones (a model provider, a registry, another owner's system) are addressed and observed only, and a change to one is a proposal to its owner. The actor framework falls under **Workbench**, since nothing in it is substrate's own and Workbench is built to leave (`workbench/BRIEF.md` section 7): each plugin a worker with its own mailbox (pgmq queue), its own role and permissions, supervision by its control plane, and asks between workers only through mailboxes with call ids, so every message that matters is recorded. | S-104 | **The first package, `packages/lookups`, and the defaults it needed** (2026-11-15; `docs/tasks/first-package.md`, under S-102 and S-103). **Step loop:** one function, `plugins/handlers/episode.py` `run(env, case, order, entries)`, used by the live run (`handlers:run`) and `driver.py`; each step every handler on the route gets `{observation, episode, step, actions}`, the first answer's value is the action, `ask` is resolved in the loop; `ladder.py` unchanged. `episode.route` builds a route from an agent file (`select: cost-order` only; a `.py` entry is made by its `make(slug, cfg, gateway, dir)`). **Live:** a source kind `items` (saved items of a type); `handlers:declare --input packages=DIR` declares a package as a stream with `episode` and its handlers as `<environment>-<name>`, in the same run as `streams.yaml` (a declare run retracts what it no longer declares), so `scripts/streams.sh` passes `PACKAGES` and `scripts/up.sh` loads each package's `plugin/` (the `question` type). An episode's steps (claims, action, handler), its end and `final` go on the input; every page read is kept as a source and, gzip-compressed, on the input (the server's checkout is read-only, so not in a file); a hand-off raises a `hand-off` decision; an episode a gateway refusal ended runs again. Saving a question does not run anything by itself: `handlers:run` is queued (MCP `run_extractor`, `scripts/streams.sh run`, or `up.sh`), since Workbench's core has no on-save trigger and is left alone. **Environment (people's custody, outside the cell):** actions `search`, `fetch`, `answer`, `hand_off`, all `free`, plus `ask` and `propose_handler` (recorded only); registries' search and page URLs read from the map's `sources` system, with page URLs given in `environment.yaml` where the map has none; a fetch outside the registries' hosts is refused, and a registry's web page (github.com/O/N, pypi.org/project/N, ...) is read as its JSON API page. The first observation is the question alone (a case's `shape`, `registry`, `name` are private). **A fetch is shown, not dumped:** a body is kept whole in the record but shown as path = value claims, breadth first, at most 2000 characters, strings cut at 100, an object or list longer than 40 shown as its size and end keys, and a `path` argument reads under one key (npm's and PyPI's full documents do not fit a 3B model's window). **Record:** JSON lines `{url, read_at, sha256, status, body}`, gzip; a judged run replays `MORPHOGEN_INPUTS/fetched.jsonl.gz` and a page not in it is an observation saying so, never a network call; `driver.py --network` records. The dev record `cases/dev.fetched.jsonl.gz` (1 MB) holds the 39 responses the dev outcomes were read from (read 2026-11-15) and 28 searches read the same day with the case's name as the query; searches with other words, other packages' pages, and sub-endpoints (npm `NAME/VERSION`, GitHub `/releases`, crates `/versions`) are not in it. **Agent (the cell's `change`):** `agent.yaml` with one model handler, `handlers/model.py` and `handlers/instructions.md`, `notes/notes.md` (empty). On the existing log (3 records, 2 verdicts) the old fields are unchanged; `reasons` gained `system: null` and `parameter_violations: []`, which the records already carried.
