| S-109 | **The first routine: rules learned by a script from the model's dev episodes, taken below the model; on dev it barely moves anything** (2026-11-15; `docs/tasks/routines.md` scope 2 and 3's first arm, under S-102 and S-103; owner: "ok go ahead with steps 1 and 2"). Built in a worktree (`.claude/worktrees/agent-a0f4cd1e115a58fdd`), not committed, not judged. **Handler** `packages/lookups/handlers/routine.py` (kind `rule`, before the model by cost order; `ladder.py` and the step loop unchanged) reads `notes/routines.yaml`: a rule is `when` (the whole question with `{name}` where it varies, the step, the kinds of the actions already taken), `do` (one `search` or `fetch` filled from the slot, or `answer` read at a `path` of a `page` fetched earlier in this episode, with `empty` for an empty field), `support` and `episodes`. It answers only when exactly one rule matches and otherwise escalates with the reason. **Learner** `packages/lookups/tools/learn_routines.py` (tooling, outside the cell's `change`, never in a judged run): replays a model-alone driver run's actions on the dev record; searches and fetches are learned from every episode, answers only where a fetch showed the dev outcome's value; a rule needs 3 agreeing episodes and none disagreeing. It refuses held-out paths. **Result:** from all 48 dev cases, 5 rules covering 4 of 21 (shape, registry) groups. **Screen** (learned on the odd 24: 1 rule; run on the even 24): correct 9 of 24 against 7 and 8 for the model alone (from shapes the routine never touched: noise), model calls 81 against 88 and 89, prompt tokens 87,455 against 94,747 and 97,888, the routine 4 of 85 steps, all default-branch. **Why so little:** 1 to 4 dev cases a group; the model's first steps disagree for the same kind of question; and the model is right under half the time, so few episodes show an answer to learn. **Found on the way, the environment's:** the GitHub page view is cut at 2,000 characters before `default_branch`, and a `fetch` with a path and no URL is refused, so that field is never shown (default-branch: 1 of 8). | S-110 | **The judge can see, on a rehearsal set; two of my "worse" candidates were not worse; every judged run here was losing its last case** (2026-11-15; `docs/tasks/routines.md` scope 1, `docs/contracts/verdict.md`'s two checks; owner: "ok go ahead with steps 1 and 2"). **The set is not held out:** the operator's cases do not exist yet, so the 48 open `dev` cases were copied to `~/.local/share/substrate/judged/lookups-dev` and named in `morphogen.local.yaml` (`cells: lookups`, with a comment saying so). Good for asking whether the judge tells same from worse; the checks are to be repeated on the operator's cases, and no real comparison is judged on this set. Base `7d16083`, cell `component:default/lookups`, 5 or 6 rounds each, Morphogen at `52385af` run on the host. **Evidence 5, same against same** (`check/lookups-same`, a comment in `agent.yaml`): fail, correct 0.4375 against 0.4375, interval -1.0% to 0.0%; tokens 193,688 against 192,413. As expected. **Evidence 4** (`check/lookups-worse`, `max_steps` 6 to 2): fail, but not worse: correct 0.458 to 0.479 (interval 0.0% to +8.3%, p 0.125), tokens 197,092 to 157,428. Run outside the judge on eight cases it fetched every time: Granite ignores that instruction while the tools are offered, so the instructions are a weak lever and the tools and the handler's code decide what it does. **Evidence 7** (`check/lookups-worse-constant`, the handler answers "yes" to everything): fail, correct 0.396 to 0.1875, interval wholly below zero (-20.8% to -20.8%). As expected: the judge refuses a worse agent. Across the four runs the base's median moved between 0.396 and 0.458. **The lost case:** a constant "yes" scores 10 of 48 outside the judge and scored 9 inside. Morphogen takes a run's last JSON line as what the run reports about itself and does not pass it to the scorer (`judge._result`; its README says so), and both judged workloads here end on a case, so the last case scored as missing in every judged run, on both sides alike: comparisons were fair, scores one case low. That is 1 of 48 for lookups and 1 of 7 for `model-handler` (S-87's 0.25 balanced accuracy was measured that way). **Fixed in the base, not in any candidate:** `packages/lookups/driver.py` and `plugins/handlers/bench/run.py` print a closing line (`{"episodes": N}`, `{"cases": N}`); `docs/contracts/environment.md` says a driver must; the contract test checks it (18 checks). This starts a new series for both cells: verdicts 3 to 7 are not comparable with later ones | adopted |
| S-111 | **Papers first: amass a catalog of what is published, present it, then read and reproduce; routines are parked** (2026-11-16; owner: "mine papers for agentic components/configurations, reproducing for our ecosystem, then anyone can mix/match for any usecase, and test/trust", then "amassing and presenting that data should be the first leg of the project"). Changes the order S-103 set; S-101 had papers first. **Why:** the first routine (S-109) learned 5 rules for 4 of 21 groups and moved nothing beyond noise, and the idea of distilling a model's repeated steps into routines is very likely published in several forms, so reading comes before building another variant. **Leg one** (`docs/tasks/paper-catalog.md`): a catalog of papers on agent harnesses and on cognitive designs with language models, filled with no model in the loop from registries and from curated lists, surveys and digests; each value a claim citing its source; a view to search it; a `testable` flag set by rule (has code, licence allows, not archived) that orders the queue for reading. **Legs two and three** are `papers-environment.md` stages A and B, working from that queue. The first topic read is our own question (routines below a model), which decides whether the parked work resumes, changes or is dropped. **Parked, not deleted:** `routines.md` and the worktree's routine handler and learner. **Kept:** the package form, the step loop, the meter, the verdict fields and the closing-line fix are what a reproduced method is judged with. Componentization is the framework's job: slots with contract tests, so existing code plugs in or is adapted, and a method without code is written from the paper as its spec. **The owner's, open:** whether a second machine runs the harvest all day (the harvest must not depend on which machine); held-out cases, still needed before any reproduction is trusted. |

A saved arXiv search is a listing too (17 of them, capped at 300 results, grouped by the part they inform: store and model, judge, loop; the owner asked for "graph world model" and "self-improving AI"). The catalog is in Postgres; every fetch is recorded under `.state/papers/` and a run can be replayed from it. **Measured:** 37 listings, 9,466 papers, 12,908 listed-in entries, 1,920 papers naming a repository, citations for all 9,466 (95 list calls, about 0.01 USD); tests 55 of 55; a repeat run writes nothing to the store. **Open:** `testable` (a repository, a permissive or LGPL licence, not archived) is checked for 93 papers, 57 pass; the other 1,827 wait on GitHub's 60 requests an hour without a login (`PAPERS_GITHUB_TOKEN`, the owner's to set). Nine searches hit their cap and nobody has read their results for relevance. The view has not been opened in a browser. `papers` is not yet in `PACKAGES` in the owner's `.env`. The harvest runs only through `scripts/workbench.sh extract` (the server mounts the repository read-only and cannot record fetches). |
| S-113 | **Which model an experiment uses is open; small and local is the preference, not a limit** (2026-11-16; owner, on my note that "testable" says nothing about a 3B model: "testing is not limited to '3B models' ... we have larger models on openrouter free tier, as well as it being an option to use claude models, i just have a strong interest in utilizing smaller/local models instead of relying entirely on larger & proprietary models for everything"). Clarifies S-97 and S-100, does not replace them: within one comparison the model is still the same on both sides, and Granite 4.0 Micro stays the starter agent's default. Across experiments the model is a choice: a larger local model on the owner's GPU, a free model at OpenRouter, or a Claude model through the gateway (metered, S-103). **For reproducing a paper's method:** try it at the smallest model first and step up only when it does not show; the smallest model at which the gain shows is recorded with the result, since that boundary moves as small models improve and is worth knowing in itself. **For the catalog:** the model a paper used is a fact to record when it is read; it is not a filter on the reading queue. |
| S-114 | **The R&D pipeline: a fixed record per paper read from its abstract, lookup by meaning, a queue, then reproduce and judge** (2026-11-16; `docs/tasks/rd-pipeline.md`; owner: parsing a whole paper into claims "is probably not optimal", "should we use some vector model to help with lookup when we want a component that does a thing, so then we can run tests on things that matter", "get the R&D pipeline up"). Dev cases are 40 abstracts with a record written by a dearer model, marked as such; held-out cases are the operator's. **The queue** is a `trial` field on `paper`. Not decided here: a scheduled daily harvest (a job on the owner's machine, theirs to approve). |
| S-115 | **The reading agent is built and scores at the floor: a small model cannot copy quotes word for word** (2026-11-16; `docs/tasks/rd-pipeline.md` steps 2 to 5, under S-114; owner: "you can batch jobs to openrouter to keep under 1000 daily calls"). **Built** in `packages/papers/`: environment (an abstract in, one `record` action, the quote gate in code), one model handler, driver and scorer, 40 dev abstracts with references written by Claude Sonnet 5.5 (marked so), `per_call` batching with the gate per paper, the record pass `papers:records`, lookup by need (`lookup.py`), the queue (`trial`), and the view's new filters; Workbench's recall gained a general `types` filter (`015_recall_types.sql`, `recall --type`, MCP `search` `types`, `POST /wb/api/recall`); `scripts/contract.py`'s environment test was generalised (papers 17 of 17, lookups still 18 of 18); tests 62 and 48. Vectors for all papers took 43 minutes on the CPU (the GPU was full with the owner's model). **Measured on dev, `ollama/granite4:3b`:** `per_call` 1: 0.2438 with 40 calls and 43,276 prompt tokens; 5: 0.2375 with 10 calls and 21,945; 10: 0.0875 with 7 calls, a quarter of the papers skipped. Floors: hand-off 0, an empty record 0.0875, always `other`/`none` 0.2438. So the default model equals the constant floor, and batching to 5 halves the tokens for under a point while 10 collapses. The cause is the gate: the model's quotes are near-copies, and 134 of its fields were refused in 40 papers. Other models did no better or could not be measured (`qwen3:4b-instruct` 0.075; free Gemma models at OpenRouter answered 429 throughout; two free reasoning models returned no record). **The record pass ran anyway** on 205 testable papers at `per_call` 5: 93 have a `component_kind`, 21 a `result`; these records are from a floor-level reader, are signed `model:ollama/granite4:3b`, and are not to be relied on. **Lookup by meaning works without records** (it found Agent Workflow Memory for reuse of past action sequences, and routing papers for cheap against dear). **Next:** the model cites sentences of the abstract by number and code takes the quote from them, so grounding no longer depends on copying; then measure again, and a larger model if still at the floor (S-113). No cell is added to `morphogen.yaml` until a reader beats the floor. |

At five a call it names the wrong `component_kind` for 23 of 40 papers and the wrong `slot` for 16. The record pass was not rerun; the 205 papers keep their earlier, unreliable record. Tests 53 and 62, contract 17 of 17, `scripts/check.sh` passes. **Next, if the owner continues this step:** name a stronger model in `packages/papers/agent.yaml` (reading abstracts once is a small cost) and measure again before any record is relied on. |

Morphogen being a self-management loop needs the catalog and can be used internally to validate/judge the catalog others pull from"; Workbench is "Postgres-CMS with first-class claims & ontologies"). **Substrate** is the harness and its slots; **Workbench** is the catalog's home (claims are the evidence, the ontology names slot types, cron runs the harvest, embeddings are lookup); **Morphogen** judges entries and the harness's own changes. | S-118 | **Handoff: the judge leads, six faults in it come first, and the plan is in `docs/handoff.md`** (2026-11-16; owner, after a review of Morphogen's concept and code: "All good points ... the CI workflow is something we could use GitHub for as a second machine", "this is the point I hand it off to you and make sure that you distill your ideas about the project and what to do so that if your model usage runs out I can switch to opus, and continue delegating to cheaper sub agents"). **The review** (read: `judge.py`'s rounds, statistics and `judge`, `evidence.py`, `runner.py`'s header, the README; not the optimizers, the structure code or the tests in depth): the concept holds (rules from the base, default deny, scores outside the candidate, the red-team ratchet). Faults: (1) isolation stops at the candidate's code, not at the agent on the same machine; (2) a whole run is one sample, which fits timing and not agent quality; (3) rounds are added while undecided with no correction, and same-against-same was checked on 12 trials; (4) nothing counts how often a private set is judged against; (5) the evidence chain has no outside anchor; (6) a run whose scorer saw too few cases is scored, not refused. **For (1), two ways, both wanted:** a GitHub Actions workflow from the base branch with private cases in a separate private repository; and on the owner's machine a second Unix user that owns the private cases and runs the judge, since a container alone does not keep the agent's own user out. **The plan** in `docs/handoff.md` wins over `docs/tasks/README.md` and `docs/direction.md` where they disagree: Morphogen fit to show, a write-up, the catalog public, held-out cases by script, one reproduction, a first audit, and only then slots and plug-ins. Each judge change still needs the owner's yes, a red-team test first and a pull request. |

After it merges, the scorers here (`packages/lookups`, `plugins/handlers/bench`) should print the two counts | adopted |
| S-120 | **Two projects, not three: Morphogen, and Substrate with Workbench as its store and GUI** (2026-11-16; owner: "maybe its all one thing with workbench as the GUI ... i found several papers and implementations around 'claim based memory' so its not terribly unique and i don't want to get too fractured", then "ok, yes"). Supersedes the positioning of Workbench as a general-purpose CMS built to leave as its own project (`workbench/BRIEF.md`, S-117's three jobs). **Why:** claim-based memory is published and implemented elsewhere, so it is not what is sold, and keeping it generic is a cost with no buyer; the judge is the part others lack, so Morphogen stays its own repository, installable alone. **What changes:** words only (`CLAUDE.md`, `README.md`, `workbench/README.md`, `workbench/BRIEF.md`'s opening, `docs/handoff.md`); no directory moves, no schema merges. **What stays:** code in `workbench/` imports nothing from the plugins, because that costs nothing. **Not changed:** `docs/spec.md` and `docs/build.yaml` (paused; the handoff wins), and two component descriptions in the map that say "built to leave with Workbench". **The owner's question about many component types** ("a chimera") is answered by one rule and no taxonomy: a component of any type is a catalog entry, a slot it fills (an interface with a contract test) and a verdict; that is handoff item 7 and waits for the first reproduction | adopted |
| S-121 | **The judge runs as a second Unix user, and its base branch moves only with the owner's password** (2026-11-16; `docs/handoff.md` item 1.1b; owner: "ok, yes"). User `morphogen-judge`, home `/var/lib/morphogen-judge` (mode 700, linger on), created by the owner. **Design:** that user holds its own Morphogen install from GitHub `main`, its own clone of the project, the request files (`morphogen request`, which exists), the held-out cases and the evidence log; the agent's user runs one wrapper through a `sudoers` rule without a password, giving a request name and a candidate commit, with the candidate as a git bundle on standard input, so neither user reads or writes the other's home; the base branch in the judge's clone advances only by a second command that is not in that rule. **Why the password:** otherwise an agent commits weaker rules, names that commit the base and is judged by them. **Known obstacle:** rootless Podman is per user, so the judge user needs the images in its own store and a gateway of its own (which also takes the model keys out of the agent's reach). **Rejected:** a container alone (the agent's user can start containers); a listening service (more to build and audit than one `sudoers` line). Not built yet | adopted |
| S-122 | **The judge's interface is "a request name and a git bundle in, a verdict out"; `sudo` carries it first, a pgmq queue in Workbench second** (2026-11-16; owner: "can we abstract the interface and maybe make it accessible via PGmq in workbench ... i'm just trying to make sure it can be automated", then "i like the plan"). Morphogen pull request 83 (merged, `b57fbbb`) is the interface: `morphogen intake judge NAME` and `advance NAME`, red team first (8 cheats), no change to the judge's rules, runner or statistics. `scripts/judge-user.sh` is the owner's setup for it here (install, clone, door, images, advance, status); only `status` has been run. **The queue, not built:** two queues (requests, verdicts), a worker run by the judge user as a systemd user service that calls the same intake function, a Postgres role that can read one queue and write the other; it lives in Substrate, so Morphogen stays installable alone. **Why a queue:** a judged run takes up to half an hour and `sudo` blocks the caller; a worker that pulls means the author runs nothing as the judge, so the `sudoers` rule can go once the worker is proven. **Limits kept:** advancing the base never goes on the queue (it stays behind the owner's password); a verdict on the queue is a copy and carries its record number and hash, the judge's evidence log is the proof (the agent has admin access to the database), which raises handoff item 1.5; messages are untrusted input like the bundle. **Order:** prove the `sudo` door, then the judge's own gateway and meter (today's meter is a script in this repository run as the author), then the worker, then GitHub Actions as the same interface a third time | adopted |
| S-123 | **Positioning: several ways in, one spine (map, cases and scorer, deploy, judge, optimize); the next build is a case-and-scorer kit; self-host first, a hosted mapper for public repositories as the free way in, hosted judging later** (2026-11-16; owner: "not all the potential customers are going to have evals, i don't even know who i'm approaching with this, which is why i was hoping to leave the field broad enough to build new agents/systems and do BPM/automation consulting work - so mapping or judging changes or deploying agentic systems with or without self-optimization, seem like the entry points... and offering a SaaS platform along with self-host so anyone can play with it means i don't just have to cold call", then "yes, record it"). **Ways in:** mapping (the loss-leader: a map, a catalog of options per slot, and which slots are worth judging), process and automation consulting, building a new agent, judging changes for teams that have evals, and self-optimization last. Each enters the spine at a different step and leaves the same assets behind. **Why a case-and-scorer kit first:** most teams have no evals, and every way in needs cases and a scorer; the kit turns an existing eval into the environment contract, and a process's history (tickets, logs, past decisions) into a case set with recorded outcomes as answers, held out by nature. **Not led with:** the safe auto-optimizer (no team lets a stranger's optimizer change its system before the judge has earned trust); red-teaming security (crowded, and it needs written scope and insurance; security faults the map finds are reported, full tests referred). Red-teaming a team's evals is offered: Morphogen's red-team list is the evidence. **The platform:** building agents is a commodity, so the judge and the slot catalog are the product; domain platforms are the same core with domain case sets, scorers and environments as the add-ons. Self-host open core first, with a quickstart (handoff item 1.6); a hosted mapper limited to public repositories and read-only access; write-ups and public audits (rehearsed on Substrate and one open-source agent project, its maintainers told first) for inbound; a hosted judge (a neutral party holding the private cases) only when someone asks to pay for it. **Before pitching:** one judged run on held-out cases and one real change that passes with a gain, and the competitor search. **Still the owner's:** the open core's licence (permissive or AGPL-like, which matters once there is a hosted service), whether consulting or the platform is the main business, pricing, the first customer | adopted |
| S-124 | **Substrate is an agent framework with Workbench as its UI; Morphogen R&D is deferred; one plan replaces the overlapping ones** (2026-11-17; owner: "we're way off on a tangent and need to clean up the overlapping docs along with the current direction... i don't know that we need to do any more R&D on morphogen right now (defer it all, we need systems to work with now) ...and i'm feeling like getting Substrate (workbench being its web UI i guess) ready as an agent framework"; on the cleanup, "Yes, archive and replace"). `docs/plan.md` is the one current plan. `docs/spec.md`, `docs/build.yaml`, `docs/direction.md`, `docs/handoff.md`, `docs/slots.md`, `docs/multi-process.md`, `docs/readiness.md`, `docs/tasks/`, `docs/writeups/` and `docs/assistant/` move to `docs/archive/` as history; with the spec gone the SessionStart hook names no step, and `check.sh` and `up.sh` skip the spec, as they already did when it was absent. Supersedes S-94's direction, S-118's handoff plan, S-123's next build (the case-and-scorer kit) and the handoff's freeze on Workbench features. Morphogen stays usable as it is. The first step is a real agent for a system the owner picks; the framework is what that agent needs. Not done here: the `build-out` skill and the spec hook are still on the map (`catalog-info.yaml` names `docs/spec.md`); retire them through Workbench and export once the stack is up (rule 8). |
| S-125 | **The framework's shape: the environment is data in Postgres, pass-through to Claude Code is the default, distilled components carry cards, the judge is an interface, environments nest** (2026-11-17; `docs/framework.md`; owner, over the session: "an agent's context should be more than just whatever text is fed to an LLM but rather an interactive environment with multiple objects/streams"; "the only way i see to make it available without opinions is if the environment can also be a straight pass-through to the LLM harness"; "every distilled component the LLM generates must carry the kind of 'intent data or KPIs or tests' that Morphogen uses"; "what are we equipping it with but a collection of tests?"; "a hierarchy of environments with a top-level orchestrator"; "an environment is just data in a database ... a Postgres-based claim log with PGgraph projection & SPARQL ontologies feels better"; "i'm depending on you to help synthesize"). **Environment:** the claim log is the source of truth; streams arrive through pgmq topics (`send_topic`, `bind_topic`, routing keys `<env>.<stream>.<kind>`; topics are in pgmq since 1.11.0 per the pgmq-cli README and docs.pgedge.com/pgmq/v1-11-1/topics, read 2026-11-17; the image pins 1.13.1); each subscriber owns a queue bound to its patterns (parallel sensing); one queue per actuator (serial acting); gates `free`/`propose`/`deny`; escalation is a topic the level above subscribes to; pgGraph, Ontop, full text and vectors are views. Reactive logic runs in handler processes, not database triggers, so each is a component with a card; this supersedes the earlier Memgraph-trigger design. **Pass-through:** Claude Code is the harness (any model through the gateway); events in by a channel (research preview, to be read and pinned before use), queries by Workbench's MCP, actions to actuator queues under the gates; it is the baseline every layer must beat. **Cards:** intent, trigger, action, source cases, KPIs, tests; a component's confidence is the judge's measured agreement on held-out cases within its trigger's scope. **Judge:** a card and candidate in, a verdict out; needs only cases the author cannot read and comparison with the incumbent; first filler a script as the judge user; Morphogen an optional filler, no new work (S-124). **Nesting:** a child environment is an object in its parent's (its streams under `<child>.#`, its actuators, its gates); the top level is the system itself (map, observer, interviews); built later, but an environment's interface is from the start exactly what a parent sees of it. Each layer is a parameter tested against pass-through. Prior art from one round of searches is cited in the page; Global Workspace Agents, CogRec and Graphiti are to be read in full first (`docs/plan.md` step 2). Open: what the LLM sees by default, the judge's runner, Morphogen's future, the first system. |
| S-126 | **Signals are not claims: a claim is an interpretation of signals by an interpreter that answers for it** (2026-11-17; `docs/framework.md`, "Signals and claims"; owner: "i'm mentally differentiating 'signals' from 'claims' where a 'claim' requires a conscious interpretation of signal(s) and maybe combines multiple signal 'sources' to provide actionable interpretation"). A signal is what a source reports, kept whole; parsing it into fields leaves it a signal. A claim cites the signals or claims it rests on and names its interpreter (the LLM, a distilled component, or a person); the test is whether it could be wrong for reasons other than a broken sensor. Streams carry signals, the claim log holds claims, actions cite claims. Refines S-125. Workbench's existing claim-per-value is read this way: a parser's value points to its signal with no interpreter; whether the schema expresses that without change is to be checked once the stack is up. |

It stays behind the judge interface (S-125) for noisy comparisons; no work on it until the owner starts the overhaul. What it reduces to (S-125): held-out cases the author cannot read, comparison with the incumbent, a verdict log; as CI components: a runner, the cases' custody, statistics as a step. Settles S-124's open question. |

| S-134 | **The harness profile (tools, prompt, MCP servers, context switches, model) is a component sized to the model; the companion starts on a lean one** (2026-11-17; `docs/framework.md`, "The harness profile"; owner: "claude code can have its tools reduced to help with smaller models?"). Read 2026-11-17 in Claude Code's CLI reference, permissions, env-vars, sub-agents and LLM gateway pages: `--tools ""` removes built-in tools from context; a bare-name deny removes a tool's schema, a scoped one only blocks calls; `--strict-mcp-config --mcp-config` loads exactly the listed servers; `--system-prompt-file` replaces the prompt (its effect on tool instructions undocumented, so tested); the `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_*` switches drop context; `--bare` and `--safe-mode` skip MCP and are not used. Model by `ANTHROPIC_MODEL`, background calls by `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL` (`ANTHROPIC_SMALL_FAST_MODEL` is not in the current docs). | S-135 | **The store is Wikibase's data model in Postgres, with OpenWiki's pinned evidence; a claim is a statement an interpreter answers for** (2026-11-17; supersedes Workbench's "every write records a claim" and settles S-126's "to be checked against the schema"; owner: "i think 'claim' ends up being redundant noise most of how its being applied right now. provenance can still be recorded and graphs projected in other ways that exhibit better data engineering/modeling", "'claim' still has its place but not ridiculously broad", "what i'm trying to move toward is something like wikidata with graph & vector features (using postgres rather than files as in langchain's openwiki+graph)"). Measured first: of 196,512 current claims, 176,318 were one parser's, 13,488 other extractors' and rules', 6,451 model handlers' and 255 people's and Claude Code's: one fact kept three times (column, claim, quote). Read 2026-11-17: Wikibase's DataModel (mediawiki.org), Wikidata's Help:Statements, Help:Ranking, Help:Property constraints portal, Help:Sources and the RDF dump format; OpenWiki (github.com/langchain-ai/openwiki, MIT; docs.langchain.com/oss/openwiki) and LangChain's post of 2026-10-28 on its evidence graph (its gains are LangChain's own, on 111 synthetic documents, unverified). **The model:** `entity` (item or property; label, description, aliases); `statement` (subject, property, a typed value: entity, string, quantity with unit and bounds, time with precision, coordinate, URL, external id; qualifiers; rank preferred, normal or deprecated); `reference` (shared; a span and who stated it: a parser's run, an interpreter and its version, a person, a rule and its premises); `span` (a source cut into paragraphs or line ranges, each with a hash and a vector); `revision` (one row per edit of an entity). The usable value is Wikidata's: preferred if any, else normal. Constraints are statements on properties, checked and reported, never blocking (Wikidata's rule). A page per entity, Markdown regenerated from its usable statements and prose, is what people and agents read; agent tools `search`, `read`, `expand`, `locate`. When a span's hash changes, the statements citing it and everything derived from them are flagged with no model call (OpenWiki). A claim is a statement whose reference is an interpreter: it enters at normal rank and the review lane prefers or deprecates it. A derived statement's reference is its rule and premises, so it can be explained and retracted. RDF through Ontop in Wikidata's two shapes (`wdt:` usable values, `p:`/`ps:`/`pq:`/`pr:` full statements); pgGraph over entity-valued usable statements; pgvector over pages and spans. Discipline that keeps it computable: typed values, properties as governed decisions, contradictions tolerated and surfaced as decisions. Built fresh (S-137), not migrated. |
| S-136 | **The companion's session runs in a container of its own, on Haiku with the owner's own Claude login through the gateway** (2026-11-17; owner: "we'll use haiku in place of local models for simplicity right now", "should be able to use whatever credentials i already have configured, or pop open a confirmation dialog", and of tmux on the host: "could that have been done with podman so substrate doesn't need to alter the host or run anything as root"). Bifrost's subscription mode (read 2026-11-17, docs.getbifrost.ai/cli-agents/claude-code): `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=<gateway>/anthropic` and `ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS="x-bf-vk: <the caller's virtual key>"`, with no `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `apiKeyHelper`; Claude Code's login token is forwarded to Anthropic and the virtual key carries governance and logging; whether the gateway needs a `providers.anthropic` entry is undocumented and tested first. The container holds Claude Code (pinned), tmux and the channel server, with its own home volume; the owner logs in once there (`/login`), since sharing the host's refreshing login between two clients can sign one out. Using Haiku loosens P3 and P4 (`docs/privacy.md`) for the companion's text stream only: the owner's messages to it reach Anthropic. Status: decided (the model, the credentials, the loosening); adopted (the container). |
| S-137 | **A fresh start: v1 is tagged and archived, and the store, the real-time environment and the companion are built anew, carrying over only what is listed** (2026-11-17; owner: "this may call for a major rewrite with a lot of schucked dead weight... might even start from scratch and bring in a few things that stay... feels like we may carry over bad engineering if not starting fresh", then "ok" to the plan). About 26k of the 36k lines of code (Workbench, the plugins, the packages) were built around per-value claims and the extractor model, both replaced (S-135). Same repository: `v1-final` tags the last v1 commit (5e2283d), the fresh tree starts on a branch with everything not carried over deleted in one commit, so history, `git blame` and this file stay. **Carried over**, each with a reason and a test: `deploy/postgres` (pgvector, pgmq, pg_cron, pgGraph), the model gateway (`scripts/gateway.py`, per-caller keys, the meter; S-77), Caddy, compose and `scripts/lib.sh` (the runtime verbs, `.env`), the tools container (nothing installed on the host), `docs/privacy.md`, `docs/framework.md`, `docs/plan.md` and this file, `lookups`' recorded cases as data, the `setup`, `research` and `upstream` skills (rewritten to the new commands). **Dropped:** Workbench's store, server, MCP and shell; the map, handlers and business plugins; the papers package's code; the extractor kit, the reasoner, trace, the contract tests, the Ontop mapping, `catalog-info.yaml` and its export (so Morphogen leaves the build while the owner refactors it); the interview and build-out skills. **Data:** both databases dumped whole to `.state/backups/` (2026-11-17) before anything changes; people's statements, open review items and interview answers come back as data imported into the new store. Order: the store (S-135), then the real-time environment (streams, actuator queues, gates, the channel), then the companion (S-136). Open, the owner's: whether the UI keeps the name Workbench. |
| S-138 | **The store's core, built as S-135 said, with these choices of its own** (2026-11-17, plan step 3b). In `store/` (schema `store/sql/001_store.sql`, applied by `scripts/db.py init`; a thin Python layer, `store/__init__.py`). **The rules live in the schema, not in callers:** a value must fit its property's datatype in Wikidata's JSON form (time as `+YYYY-MM-DDT00:00:00Z` with precision 0 to 14, quantity with amount, unit item and bounds, coordinate, URL, external id, string, item, property; `somevalue` and `novalue` as snaks), or the write fails; every statement cites at least one reference, checked at commit (stricter than Wikidata, so a claim can always be told from a parser's output); a statement's value and qualifiers are fixed once written and only its rank changes (a new belief is a new statement, the old one deprecated with a reason); nothing is deleted but a statement's link to a reference (re-citing after review); every write names its author (`store.actor`, else refused) and gives one revision per entity per transaction with a full snapshot. **Spans are pinned:** a span is immutable; a read of its source with other text makes a new span and supersedes the old, a missing locator supersedes it as gone, so what was cited stays readable. **Flags are computed, not stored** (`store.flag`, a recursive view): statements citing a superseded span, then by rule premises everything derived from them, with no model call; re-citing the new span clears the statement and its derivations. Usable values are a view (`store.usable`); constraints are statements on properties with four kinds to start (single value, format, one of, value type through instance-of and subclass-of), reported by `store.violation`, never blocking. Labels are one language for now. **Pages** are rendered in SQL (`store.render`), always current; `store.page` caches them for full-text and trigram search. **Agent tools** `search`, `read`, `expand`, `locate` over MCP (`store/mcp.py`, in `.mcp.json`): a stdio JSON-RPC server on the standard library and psycopg rather than an SDK (four read tools need no more), speaking 2025-11-25 and 2025-06-18 (read 2026-11-17, modelcontextprotocol.io/specification/2025-11-25; the current 2026-07-28 drops the `initialize` handshake, which clients still send), in a read-only session. It connects as `postgres` for now; a role of its own comes with the UI. **Imported from v1** (`store/import_v1.py`, `scripts/db.py import-v1`, idempotent by each reference's note of its v1 id): 274 claims by a person (w1-editor) or Claude Code, as 395 statements (an object becomes a statement per key, a list of components item-valued statements), and 45 decisions (the 44 open ones and the one the owner decided); v1 recorded no interview answers. **The graph** (`store/sql/002_graph.sql`): pgGraph straight over `store.entity` and `store.statement`, nothing copied (owner: "the idea of PGgraph is that it works over existing tables so you don't need to keep anything in sync"); a statement's value edge is labelled with its rank and `store.path` follows only `subject`, `normal` and `preferred` edges, so deprecated statements drop out as their rank changes, through pgGraph's own triggers. A normal statement outranked by a preferred one is still an edge (pgGraph filters a row at a time; the best rank is a rule across rows, and stays exact in `store.usable`). Read 2026-11-17 from the installed 1.2.1's own functions; row-predicate subgraphs are unsupported in it (PG018), and a node filter applies to entities too, so neither could exclude deprecated statements. **JIT is off** for the store's database and its render and touch functions: the planner overestimates the recursive flag view by orders of magnitude, and compiling it for every statement on a page made a write take half a second (the suite went from over five minutes to 15 seconds). Tested by `scripts/tests/test_store.py` (52 checks in a throwaway database). **Not yet, still step 3b:** Ontop's RDF in Wikidata's two shapes, and vectors on spans and pages (the columns exist; the embedding model is picked with the `research` skill). | adopted |
| S-139 | **Maps, roles, interfaces and catalogs come back as a small vocabulary in the store, MCP write tools and two skills; the judge plugs in as a verdict** (2026-11-17, inside plan step 3b; owner: "i think the concepts i may have let slip away from the earlier build: components & catalogs & maps ... a system mapping itself as a component graph and cataloging alternative components which fit slots/roles/interfaces ... and that's where an external project 'Morphogen' ties in to judge that the change actually scores higher on measurable goals ... its actually pretty simple and just needs some skill files and/or MCP tooling", and of generalising it, "a basic cognitive ability to deconstruct entities into sub-entities and understand how they work together along with the patterns/principles that make things interchangeable"; then "Now, inside 3b"). Any thing, software or not, in Wikidata's terms where they exist (`store/sql/003_components.sql`): a whole *has part* (P527) a part, qualified by *object has role* (P3831); a role *requires interface*, a part or candidate *provides interface*; a candidate's *catalog id* qualified by *catalog*, *described at URL* (P973); a whole *has goal*, a goal's *metric* and *target*; a *measurement* qualified by goal and *point in time* (P585); a *verdict* (better, worse, no measurable difference) qualified by *for role*, *against* and goal, whose reference is the judge. Value-type constraints keep roles, interfaces, goals, catalogs and verdicts apart. `store.alternatives(role)`: what provides every interface a role requires, and what fills it now. **MCP writes** (`store/mcp.py`): `create_item` (answering with similar items already there), `say` (evidence as a pinned span, `store.read_span`), `deprecate`; beside `path`, `alternatives` and `violations`. A client's write is a claim: its reference is the client as interpreter with the name and version it gave at initialize, at normal rank; it deprecates only its own statements, and a person reviews the rest; properties are not made over MCP (governed, S-135). **Skills:** `map` (take a thing apart into parts, roles and interfaces, with evidence) and `catalog` (find candidates for a role in more than one catalog, write what their sources say, measure; a verdict is a judge's), and `research` rewritten as the catalog skill applied to Substrate, its v1 tools being gone. Morphogen stays out of the build (rule 9) until the owner's refactor; it comes in as a judge whose verdicts are statements. Tested in `scripts/tests/test_store.py` (64 checks). | decided |
| S-140 | **Maps seen as diagrams: the UI's first page, Cytoscape.js with dagre for the diagram, and maps as a query** (2026-11-17, plan step 3b; owner: "make sure we can see maps as a graph/diagram in the UI"). **Substrate mapped** with the `map` skill (its first use): Substrate (Q10) has the store (`store/`, with Postgres, `store.mcp` and the embedding model as its parts), the gateway, Ollama, the tools container, Podman compose and now the UI, each with its role and the interfaces roles require and parts provide. **Maps as a query** (`store/sql/004_map.sql`): `store.map(whole, depth)` gives the edges (has-part, plays, requires, provides, could-fill from `store.alternatives`, each with whether it is flagged), `store.map_nodes` the nodes by kind (whole, part, role, interface, candidate); only usable statements are followed. **The diagram library** (research skill; role Q144 in the store, the trial in `scripts/trials/map-diagram/`): candidates from GitHub topics and npm, read through deps.dev 2026-11-17; each drew Substrate's real map (28 nodes) and a synthetic one (121 nodes) in headless Chromium (Playwright 1.64.1) under the UI's CSP. **Picked: Cytoscape.js 3.34.4 with cytoscape-dagre 4.0.1** (both MIT; 481 KB; 53 ms and 173 ms; no overlapping nodes; long labels wrap), vendored in `ui/static/vendor` (SHA-256 in its `VENDOR.md`, checked by the tests). It injects one rule as an inline style element unless an element with id `__________cytoscape_stylesheet` exists, so the page links that rule from `ui/static/cytoscape.css` under that id and the CSP stays `script-src 'self'; style-src 'self'`. **Lost:** AntV G6 5.1.1 (MIT, CSP-clean, but 1.4 MB, about 1 s to draw, and cuts long labels short); vis-network 10.1.4 (Apache-2.0 or MIT, fastest, but its standalone build injects 14 inline style elements and its edges tangle on a real map; its peer build is untested); Mermaid 12.2.0 (its package says MIT, but its bundle carries ELK, Eclipse Public License: `.state/trials/map-diagram/bundles.txt`); elkjs 0.12.1 (EPL-2.0 or GPL-3.0). Not looked at closely: sigma.js (no layered layout, from memory, not checked) and d3-dag (needs d3 and more of our own drawing). The owner may prefer G6; a judge would decide on the goal Q154 once one is in the build. **The UI** (`ui/server.py`, `ui/static`; its name is still the owner's): a standard-library Python server, compose service `ui` on the tools image, read-only and without capabilities, seeing only `ui/`, on 127.0.0.1:`UI_PORT` (8100), with a read-only database session; search, a page per entity, and a map per whole at `#/map/<id>`. The diagram hides a provides edge the part's role already requires (a toggle shows every edge), draws a role's interface its filler does not provide as missing (red) and a flagged statement in orange; clicking a node opens its page. Caddy in front comes later. **Found on the way:** quoting different excerpts of one line supersedes the earlier span and flags what cites it, and hand-typed locators were wrong in 9 of 32 citations; so `store.stale_file_spans` reports cited file lines no longer at their locator (and where a moved line went), `check.sh` counts them, `check.sh` counts flags only on statements in use, and the `map` skill says to copy quotes from the file; and `store/mcp.py` now reconnects after Postgres restarts. | S-151 | **The first closed loop: components with cards, an author, a judge outside the author's reach, and the owner's decision** (2026-11-18; owner, after asking what the framework is: "yes, close the loop with the voice head first"). **Schema** (`store/sql/007_components.sql`): `env.component` keeps every version of a component with its card (intent, trigger, action, params, KPIs, tests, source cases, rationale) and a status (proposed, judging, judged, refused, active, retired; one active per name); `env.verdict` keeps a judge's result whole (each KPI for candidate and incumbent, case counts, every case's output); `env.held_out` puts a fixed third of the record (by a hash of the signal id) out of an author's reach; `env.training_turns` is what an author may read (heard and said paired, with timings, interruptions, handoffs). An author (`env_agent`) submits and reads the training slice; the judge (`env_judge`, login `judge`) claims, reads everything, records verdicts and files an activation as a proposal; only a person activates, through an approved `companion.activate`. **The first component** is the voice head's reply (`voice/reply.py`, shared by the voice head and the judge so both run the same thing): Gemma's instructions and settings, seeded as version 1 from S-149's prompt; every `companion.voice.said` names the version that produced it. **The author** (`environment/author.py`, in the companion's container, the `improve` actuator or the page's Improve voice button): the local Gemma, so what was said aloud stays here (P3), thinking off (with it, medium, Gemma thought for 460 s, filled 8000 tokens and never answered; streamed, since the gateway cuts a request at 300 s). **The judge** (`environment/judge.py`, compose service `judge`, its own login and gateway key `judge:voice`, local only): the 30 labelled utterances in `judge/cases/voice_reply.jsonl` (mounted in its container only), the held-out voice turns and the card's own tests, each run for candidate and incumbent in turn; KPIs: handoff agreement, the share fit to speak, median time to the first word, own tests; worse if below by more than one case, much slower or failing its own tests; better only if above by more than one case. **First run:** the author proposed version 2 (temperature 0.5, at most 150 tokens, an added line about not attempting complex answers). Verdict 1 said better on handoffs 29/30 against 28/30, but that is one case: the judge compared rounded rates, and rounding pushed a one-case gap over the margin; fixed to compare counts unrounded, with a test for that case. The same card judged again under the fixed rule (version 3, verdict 2) came out the same (29/30 for both: the one case was noise), and was refused. Verdict 1 is kept whole; its activation proposal (action 24) waits for the owner, who should refuse it. Seen on the way: the companion's replies read aloud are written for reading (lists, "GiB"), the next component worth a card. Not yet: more than one run per case (so smaller differences can be told from noise), an LLM or the owner as a judge of answer quality, the store's verdict statements (S-139) written from `env.verdict`, the lifecycle's demotion, and the rate of model calls per event. | decided |

In the voice head Kokoro speaks a sentence while the model writes the next, so the "during" numbers are the ones that count: the GPU is 0.25 s faster to first audio and about 0.9 s faster a sentence, for no extra cost to the model. VRAM with both: 4.1 of 8 GB. **So:** `TTS_URL=http://tts:8880` stays (the voice head has used it since S-150), Kokoro on the CPU is the fallback when the GPU one does not answer. | S-153 | **Substrate is the framework we show; the Knowledge Kernel is its store; MetaSoar is its cognitive layer** (2026-11-20; owner, asked which of Substrate and MetaSoar is the framework to advertise: "Substrate"; asked the kernel's role: "Kernel is the store"; and earlier the same day: "i want to see that as something anyone can use, and we can advertise as our framework for AI"). Three kernel asks go to the owner when the step reaches them (`current_edge`, D-86, already approved by D-87; a free-text reason on a status change; review forced on `set_status`). Until step 3d's last item, `store/` stays in service | decided |
| S-154 | **The UI, the skills and the tests read the store from the Knowledge Kernel** (2026-11-21; plan step 3d's item "the UI, the skills and the tests repointed", after S-153 and the kernel's D-103, D-104 and D-105; the kernel's repository was not changed). **What moved:** (a) **The UI** (`ui/server.py`) reads the entity list, search, entity page and map from the kernel's `substrate` schema (`substrate.wholes`, `search`, `page`, `map_nodes`, `map`; the kernel's view-substrate module) as the module's role `kkm_view_substrate`, on a read-only session, so the kernel's row-level security applies to every answer; ids stay Q numbers (`urn:kk:substrate:Q10` in the kernel), the edge's `statement` is the claim's id, `flagged` is false until the span-change watcher exists. The companion's page and its two writes (the `env` schema) stay on the substrate database. The old path stays behind one setting, `STORE_BACKEND=store`; the default is `kernel`. (b) **The setting:** `KERNEL_URL` in `.env` (empty in `.env.example`): the kernel's database as the UI reaches it; empty means the database `kernel` in the same Postgres as the kernel's own login `kk_authenticator`, whose password is a new generated secret (`KK_AUTHENTICATOR_PASSWORD`, with `KK_JWT_SECRET` for the kernel's tokens), read through `env_value` like the others (`scripts/db.py kernel_url`, `ui/server.py kernel_url`). One Postgres, two databases (`substrate`, `kernel`): answers the open question in `docs/store-to-kernel.md` and keeps `STORE_URL` as it is. (c) **The skills** (`map`, `catalog`, `research`, `setup`) use the kernel's MCP server in the `substrate` profile, registered in `.mcp.json` as `kernel`, so the tools are `mcp__kernel__*`; they say what differs from the old tools (D-105: a write waits for the validator and may be pending; long Q numbers for new items; claim ids and `#handles` for statements; no `violations`; quantities amount-only; belief shown beside each line), and that `stale_file_spans` and the flagging of a changed span await the span-change watcher. (d) **Deploy** (not tried here: no Podman on the machine this was written on): the kernel is a second database in the same Postgres; its image is built from `repos/kernel` (`scripts/build.sh kernel`, `repos.yaml` entry `kernel`, on the branch with the Substrate profile until the kernel's pull requests 117 and 118 merge); compose services `kernel-init` and `kernel-export` (profile `kernel-admin`, run by `scripts/kernel.sh init` and `export`) and `kernel` (the validator, `kk serve --only validator`, as `kk_authenticator`); `scripts/kernel.sh login NAME` makes an agent's login (kept in `.state/kernel/`, mode 600) and its `.mcp.json` entry, `scripts/kernel.sh mcp NAME` is the stdio server in the kernel's image on the host network, as you; `scripts/up.sh` makes the database, installs the schema and exports; `scripts/check.sh` has a kernel line. | S-157 | **The catalog is the interface; executors are catalog entries, one per layer of a map** (2026-11-21; owner: "the agent would produce little reusable code blocks that get strung together in rules engines ... i don't know if i want to marry a particular execution engine when the real interface is having a catalog of components that can be assembled whether they are workflow blocks, scripts/commands, language libraries, services", then "there are multiple 'executors' that can handle different parts or layers of a graph/map. programs could be decompiled into call graphs, microservice structures into service topologies"). (a) **Blocks:** what an agent writes (a script, a function, a rule, a service call) is a catalog entry like any other part (S-139): the interface it provides, as a WoT Thing Description (inputs, outputs, transport, the kernel's choice for runtime interfaces), and its **effects**: whether it changes anything outside itself, whether it is safe to retry, its timeout. A block that changes the world runs behind a gate (`free`, `propose`, `deny`). Each block has a card and is judged (S-151) before it is activated. (b) **Assemblies** are data: a P-Plan graph of catalog entries, stored as claims, not in any engine's format. (c) **Executors** are catalog entries too (a plain step runner, a rules engine, a BPMN engine, Compose, Kubernetes, a language runtime), each attached to a layer of the map (process, service, code, device), and a step that crosses layers is where one hands over to the next. Which executor runs a layer is chosen from the catalog and can be swapped. (d) **Maps come from encoders, one per layer:** code to call graph, compose files, catalogs and traces to service topology (the kernel's `map-drift` compares declared against observed), logs to processes (the kernel's process mining). (e) **One small default executor** ships as a module (the kernel's D-36: never in the kernel), so blocks can be tested as they are written. Why: effects are where engines differ; declared on the interface, any executor can honour them, and a small local model picks from a dozen typed, judged blocks far more reliably than it writes a workflow. First check: the agent writes two blocks, catalogs them with interfaces and effects, assembles them, the default executor runs the plan, and the judge scores each block against a hand-written one. |

| S-162 | **The offline agent's loop is our own: `mcp` + `openai`, about 200 lines; no agent framework** (2026-11-22; research skill; role: the loop of an agent on the local model `local/gemma` (Gemma 4 26B-A4B on llama.cpp, through the gateway, 16k context, about 21 tokens/s); MetaSoar M-17: the model is called only at impasses, compiled skills run without it). **Evidence is in the trial output and this row, not in the store:** the kernel store's MCP tools are not available in the session that did this research, so no claims were written; write them when the kernel is reachable (pick *has part*, rejected candidates with the reasons below). **Need:** OpenAI chat completions with tool calls against our base URL and key; MCP client over stdio (the kernel's `memory` profile: recall, remember, explain, changes_since); our code decides when the model is called and counts the calls; few dependencies, MIT/BSD/Apache, release at least two weeks old, pinned exactly, in a container, no cloud service, no telemetry on by default. **Pick, pinned** (`scripts/trials/offline-agent/own-requirements.txt`, resolved with `uv pip install --exclude-newer 2026-11-08` so nothing is under two weeks old): `mcp==2.3.0` (MIT, 2026-10-02) and `openai==3.46.0` (Apache-2.0, released before 2026-11-08; 3.57.0 is from 2026-11-20 and too new), 31 distributions, 59 MB. Read: PyPI JSON API `https://pypi.org/pypi/<pkg>/json` and deps.dev `api.deps.dev/v3/systems/pypi/packages/<pkg>/versions/<v>` on 2026-11-22. Note that `mcp` 2.x renamed FastMCP to `MCPServer` (`mcp.server.mcpserver`) and the client's `Tool.inputSchema` to `input_schema`. **Candidates (PyPI and deps.dev):** (1) pydantic-ai-slim 2.71.0, MIT, 2026-11-19, 70 requirement lines with the `openai` and `mcp` extras, 60 installed distributions, 122 MB; passes the case and its model call can be gated by subclassing `OpenAIChatModel.request`, but its agent loop issues the follow-up model call itself, so skipping a call means an exception out of the run, not a substitute answer, and the release is under two weeks old. (2) openai-agents 0.25.0, MIT, 2026-11-18, 56 requirement lines, 38 installed distributions, 73 MB; passes, gate by subclassing `OpenAIChatCompletionsModel.get_response`, same limit on skipping, **and it connected to `api.openai.com:443` (its tracing export) as soon as `OPENAI_API_KEY` was in the environment, with the model pointed at the stub**; the off switch is `OPENAI_AGENTS_DISABLE_TRACING=1`, so it would be telemetry on by default; also under two weeks old. (3) smolagents 1.26.0, 2026-05-29: PyPI gives no licence field and deps.dev lists none, so rule 7 cannot be met from the registries; its default agent is a code-writing loop (model output is executed), the opposite of compiled skills; not trialled. (4) mcp-agent 0.2.6, Apache-2.0, 2025-12-05: 36 requirement lines including scikit-learn, numpy, fastapi and OpenTelemetry OpenAI/Anthropic instrumentation and an OTLP exporter; not trialled. (5) LangGraph 1.2.24, MIT, 2026-11-20 (under two weeks): 6 requirements but needs `langchain-core` and a `langchain-openai` model class; graph nodes could gate calls, but it adds the LangChain stack for a loop of about 40 lines; not trialled. (6) own loop: `mcp` + `openai` (`agent_own.py`, 45 lines for the case; the full loop with counting, budgets and a skill table is the 200 lines). **Why the own loop wins:** M-17 makes the decision to call the model the heart of the loop. In the own loop `gate()` runs before each call and may answer without calling, so a compiled skill returns its result with no model call; in both frameworks the loop owns the call, and the gate can only refuse (raise). It is also the smallest, has no tracing code, and each dependency is one we already pin or would need for the MCP client. **Trial** (`scripts/trials/offline-agent/run.sh`; stub OpenAI server and stub stdio MCP server with one tool `add`; run behind a recording proxy that refuses and logs anything leaving 127.0.0.1, with `OPENAI_API_KEY=stub-key` set; output `.state/trials/offline-agent/summary.txt`); case `tool`: task, model asks for `add`, tool result back, final answer "The answer is 5."; case `veto`: our gate refuses the second model call. Lines: `own tool pass stub_model_calls=2 extra_network=none installed=31 dists 59MB`; `own veto vetoed, stub_model_calls=1, none`; `pai tool pass calls=2 network=none 60 dists 122MB`; `pai veto vetoed calls=1 none`; `oai tool pass calls=2 network=api.openai.com:443 38 dists 73MB`; `oai veto vetoed calls=1 network=api.openai.com:443`. **llama.cpp b12098 (ghcr `server-cuda12-b12098`, our `LLM_IMAGE`), read at `raw.githubusercontent.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/b12098/common/common.h` line 655:** `bool use_jinja = true;`. In `common/arg.cpp` only the completion and mtmd examples reset it (`params.use_jinja = false;   // disable jinja by default`, lines 1348 and 1350), the server does not; the option is `--jinja` / `--no-jinja` (line 3666, env `LLAMA_ARG_JINJA`). So the server has Jinja templates, and tool calls, by default, and `--jinja` in `LLM_ARGS` is not needed; `deploy/compose.yaml` (line 254) passes `--jinja` on the command line anyway, so `.env.example` is unchanged. **Bifrost** (pinned `docker.io/maximhq/bifrost:v2.3.2`, `GATEWAY_IMAGE` in `.env.example`; source read at tag `transports/v2.3.2`): `core/schemas/chatcompletions.go` has `Tools []ChatTool` and `ToolChoice` on the request (lines 224-225) and `ToolCalls []ChatAssistantMessageToolCall` on the assistant message (lines 1589, 1855); `core/providers/openai/chat.go` forwards the tools, rewriting only `custom` tools to `function` for models older than GPT-5. Our `local` provider is `custom_provider_config` with `base_provider_type: openai` and `allowed_requests` chat_completion and chat_completion_stream (`scripts/gateway.py`), so tools and tool calls pass through to llama.cpp. Not run against the live stack here (no gateway in this session): the first run with the real model checks one tool call end to end. **Next:** a module under `environment/` or a new `agent/` with the loop and the `memory` profile client when a plan step needs it. | adopted |
| S-163 | **The worker: an offline agent on the local model** (2026-11-22; plan step 1, on S-162's pick). **Built:** `environment/worker.py`, environment `worker`. `run_task(task, model, tools, gate)` is the loop: before every model call it asks `gate(state)`, and a string from the gate is the answer with no model call (**M-17's seam: a compiled skill answers here; the default gate returns None**); the loop counts `model_calls` and `skipped_calls` itself, stops at `WORKER_MAX_STEPS` (8) with ok false, trims old tool results to `WORKER_CONTEXT_TOKENS` (11000 of the 16k, estimated at 3.5 characters a token), cuts each tool result at 4000 characters, and returns `cited`: the claim ids (uuids or `#handles`) in the answer that a tool result also showed, so an invented id does not count. **Model:** the `openai` client at `GATEWAY_URL/v1` with the caller's key (`WORKER_KEY`, header `x-bf-vk`) and `WORKER_MODEL` (`local/gemma`), a call id header per call. **Tools:** the kernel's `memory` profile over stdio (`kernel-mcp` run by the worker with `KK_PROFILE=memory` and its own kernel login, `.state/kernel/worker.env`, host rewritten to `db:5432`; only recall, remember, explain and changes_since are offered), and `read_file`, `list_dir`, `write_file` (confined to `WORKER_WORKSPACE`: the path is resolved and refused if it lands outside, so `..`, absolute paths and links out fail; 100,000 characters at most), `now` and `ask_companion`. **Environment:** queue `worker_loop` on `worker.owner.message` and `worker.task`; actuators `worker.reply` (free) and `worker.ask_companion` (**propose**: it hands work to the cloud, docs/privacy.md P3; the tool tells the model "proposed, waiting for the owner", and once the owner approves, `run` publishes it as `companion.owner.message`); each outcome is a `worker.task.done` signal {text, ok, model_calls, skipped_calls, steps, cited, task} and a reply. The worker's database login `worker` is made by `scripts/db.py init` when `WORKER_DB_PASSWORD` is set. **Gateway:** caller `worker:loop`, provider `local` only, key `WORKER_KEY`. **Compose:** service `worker` (profile `llm`; read_only, cap_drop ALL, no-new-privileges, tmpfs /tmp, the named volume `substrate_worker-workspace` at /workspace), image `substrate-worker:local` from `deploy/worker/Containerfile`: **the kernel's image (python 3.13.16 on Alpine, pinned by digest there; it has `kernel-mcp`, `mcp==2.3.0`, psycopg) plus `openai==3.46.0`, `jiter==0.19.0`, `sniffio==1.3.1` from `deploy/worker/requirements.txt`**, because the MCP server must run inside the worker's container and only the kernel's image has it; build the kernel first (`scripts/build.sh worker`, also part of `build.sh` with no argument). `scripts/up.sh` puts an empty `.state/kernel/worker.env` in place before the services start and, after the kernel's schema, runs `scripts/kernel.sh login worker --no-mcp-json` (new flag: the login without replacing the `kernel` entry in `.mcp.json`). **UI:** `#/worker` (GET `/api/worker`, POST `/api/worker/say`, `/api/worker/decide`): send a task, the log with model calls, skipped calls and cited ids, and the hand-offs waiting for the owner. **Settings (`.env.example`, empty or defaulted):** `WORKER_KEY`, `WORKER_DB_PASSWORD` (both made by `scripts/settings.sh` from `deploy/secrets.txt`), `WORKER_MODEL`, `WORKER_MAX_STEPS`, `WORKER_CONTEXT_TOKENS`, `WORKER_REASONING`. **Tested here** (`scripts/tests/test_worker.py`, 28 checks without a database, in a venv with `mcp==2.3.0`, `openai==3.46.0`, psycopg 3.3.6): a tool round trip through a real stdio MCP server, the cited id, only memory tools offered, the gate skipping a call (model not called, skipped counted; after a tool step; a failing gate), the step limit, a failing model call, workspace escapes refused (`..`, absolute, link; write outside), context trimming, made-up ids, bad arguments and unknown tools, the login's host rewrite, and the `openai` client against the trial's stub server (two calls, the key, the tools). The database checks (setup, the queue, task.done, reply applied, ask_companion left proposed and reaching the companion only once approved, the worker unable to decide its own proposal) are in the same file behind `ADMIN_URL`; **they did not run here** (no pgmq in this container) and run with `scripts/test.sh`, which now runs this file in the kernel's image. **Not yet run on the owner's machine:** one tool call end to end through the gateway (Bifrost forwards `tools` and `tool_calls` by its source, S-162, and llama.cpp b12098 enables Jinja by default, but neither was exercised here), the image build, and the done-when of step 1: network off, a task from the UI done with a cited recall and a remembered result, every call in the gateway's log. | adopted |
| S-164 | **Blocks, assemblies and the default executor** (2026-11-22; plan step 3, S-157 a-c, MetaSoar's M-17: what the agent solved is compiled into skills that run without the model). **Built:** `blocks/`. A block is a directory with `block.yaml` (or `block.json`; PyYAML 6.0.3 is already pinned in the tools image) and its code: name, version, description; `interface` inputs and outputs as JSON Schema, kept to the subset type, properties, required, items, enum (the shape of a WoT Thing Description action's input and output); `effects` changes_world, idempotent, timeout_s; `invoke` kind `python` (module:function, run with `python -I` in a subprocess, JSON on stdin and stdout), `command` (argv, JSON on stdin) or the declared kinds `http` and `mcp`, which the executor refuses with a message. Blocks are validated on load by a stdlib validator. A plan is JSON in P-Plan's terms (Plan, steps, variables): each step names a block and maps its inputs from plan variables (`$input.x`), earlier steps (`$step.field`) or literals (`{"value": ...}`); order comes from those references; validation refuses a missing block, a type mismatch, an unmapped required input, an unknown source, a cycle and a trigger group that is not a variable. `blocks/run.py` is the default executor (manifest `blocks/executor.yaml`: layer `code`, runs python and command, refuses http and mcp). **It enforces:** inputs and outputs against the block's interface, `timeout_s` (the subprocess is killed), one retry and only for an idempotent block, and that a `changes_world` block runs only through the Env actuator `blocks.<name>` (`Env.act`): `free` runs it, `propose` stops the run as `waiting` (resume with the earlier record: finished steps are kept, the proposal is not repeated), `deny` fails it, and with no environment it is refused; the action is closed with `Env.done`. The run record (per step: input, output, duration, ok, attempts, status) is returned as JSON, and `Executor.publish` writes it as a `blocks.run.done` signal. `Env.status(action)` is new for the resume. **The skill gate** `skill_gate(catalog)` is `worker.run_task`'s `gate`, M-17's first rung: a task that matches a plan's `trigger.pattern` (a regex whose named groups are the plan's variables) is run by the plan and its answer returned with no model call; a failed plan or no match returns None, so the model is called; a plan waiting on the owner answers with that notice. Examples in `blocks/examples/`: `word_count` (pure), `write_note` (changes the workspace) and the plan `note_with_count`. **Tested** (`scripts/tests/test_blocks.py`, 40 checks, no database or network; the worker's 26 still pass): block and plan validation good and bad, order, the record, timeout, retry rules, refused kinds, the gate's three answers, and the gate through `run_task` with a model stub that fails if called (model_calls 0, skipped_calls 1). **Not built yet:** cards and judging of blocks (S-151), blocks and plans as catalog claims in the kernel (they are files), the `http` and `mcp` kinds, wiring `skill_gate` into `worker run` (it passes `None` now), and S-157's first check with the real model writing the blocks. |
