| S-176 | **The catalog entry: Substrate's parts as Backstage entity files, read into the kernel by encoder-backstage; a fit check over files, compose and the kernel** (2026-11-22; plan step 1 of S-174; the kernel's D-101 first content type, D-123). **Format chosen, not invented:** Backstage's entity format (`backstage.io/v1alpha1`), which the kernel already reads (`encoder-backstage`, the kernel's D-100) and `map-drift` already compares with observed calls; one file per entity in `catalog/` (47: 22 components including jobs, agents, blocks, a plan and two external services, 22 APIs, the database, the system, the owner), so each file is one item's version. Interfaces are `API` entities, `spec.type` the protocol (`openapi`, `asyncapi` for pgmq queues, `mcp`, `websocket`, `http`, `wot-td`), `spec.definition` the contract (a block's is a WoT Thing Description action from its `block.yaml`); licence, image, compose service and decision as `substrate.dev/*` annotations. A queue is provided by the component that receives on it (AsyncAPI's sense). **`scripts/catalog.py`:** `check` (every consumed API has a provider, every reference resolves, compose matched both ways), `apis`, and `check --kernel` (the same fit from the kernel's usable claims under the read model's role, `kkm_view_substrate`, and drift between files and kernel). **Into the kernel:** compose job `kernel-catalog` (profile kernel-admin; `scripts/kernel.sh catalog`), the volume `kernel-artifacts` as the artifact store. **Seen:** 47 change sets applied; every claim cites its file and line (each file an artifact, `application/yaml`); 45 provides and consumes links, no gap, no drift; a second run submitted only the one new file. **Found on the way:** the first entries modelled the worker's queue backwards and missed two compose jobs, both caught by the check; my own `kernel-catalog` job was flagged until it had an entry. `scripts/check.sh` now runs the check; `scripts/tests/test_catalog.py` (9 checks). **For the kernel, follow-ups:** `encoder-backstage` reports every cross-file reference as `dangling-ref` (it resolves within one file, though the claims point at the right IRIs), and writes no current-version link from an item to its artifact (D-101's `kkt:hasArtifact`, or a standard term, `docs/content.md`'s open question); D-101's per-type items view is not built yet. | adopted |
| S-177 | **Compose into the kernel and back: the kernel's `encoder-compose` and `decoder-compose`** (2026-11-22; plan step 2 of S-174; the kernel's D-125, PR OpenReplicant/kernel#134, stacked on #133). Built as kernel modules, not in Substrate, because the kernel's architecture puts encoders and decoders in modules and Compose is any distribution's concern; they generalise the self-encoder's `encode_compose` (which reads only the kernel's own files). A compose file becomes a service template whose version is the file (D-101) and node templates `urn:kk:node:<service>` with node type, image, profiles, ports, volumes, environment variable names (never values) and dependencies, each claim citing its lines; the decoder renders the file from that version and the current claims, so a changed claim changes the file. **Seen in this stack's kernel:** `deploy/compose.yaml`'s 17 services became 242 claims and decoded back equal as compose reads it; the catalog's components and compose's services meet on the same nodes (the voice head: 20 claims from the catalog, 28 from compose). **Found and fixed:** the catalog called the database `postgres` and compose `db`, so their nodes did not meet: the entry is now `resource:db`; the old file's claims stayed current until the kernel encoders gained `--prune` (retract what removed or renamed files said); a run from another working directory re-keyed every file (456 claims retracted and re-added, nothing lost), so the `kernel-catalog` job now passes `--root /`. **Not yet:** a `scripts/kernel.sh compose` job (it waits for a published kernel image with the modules), running state encoded back (`compose ps` as observations), and the decoder writing the stack's compose file in use. | adopted |
| S-178 | **M-17's gate at the model endpoint: a proxy in front of the gateway, asking decision questions; tabular in-context learning over embeddings as the first online learning** (2026-11-22; the owner: "the proxy for sure, and that makes it more universal so we're not just tied to bifrost"; "the tabular system is a first implementation of online learning at this point in the architecture (there are alternative ways to do this but i like how we're learning with embeddings)"; plan step 3). (a) **A proxy, not a Bifrost plugin:** it speaks OpenAI chat completions and Anthropic Messages (so Claude Code's calls pass it too), streaming included, and forwards what it does not answer to the gateway, which still records every model call (S-77). (b) **Every model call is a decision point**, not only a task's first message (Soar compiles decisions, not whole tasks): a skill may answer with final text or with a tool call. (c) **The gate's interface is the System One contract** (TypeSafe's Jev, 2026-09-15, and its open replications, `github.com/andyrewlee/awesome-system-one`, read 2026-11-22): a state and typed questions (yes/no, choice, score) in, a calibrated probability per option out; the gate skips the model only above a threshold and otherwise falls through. **Implementations are pluggable, to be picked and measured with the research skill:** (1) next-token probabilities read from the fast model already running (LFM3 1.7B), the free baseline; (2) a small open decision model (candidates as reported, unverified: decider 0.8B and dohnuts 0.8B, Apache-2.0; NanoJev 0.6B, MIT; Laya 421M, Apache-2.0; d1, licence unstated); (3) **embeddings (text, audio, image or feature detectors such as Silero's VAD or M-18's encoder) with a calibrated tabular in-context head** (TabICLv2, BSD-3-Clause code; weights' terms to check; TabPFN 2.5+ and Google TabFM weights are non-commercial, so not defaults), whose context is the gate's own log of labelled routes: online learning with no training step, and the way to non-text senses. S-170's embedding vote (12/12, 65 ms) is the baseline to beat. (d) **Every routed call is logged** with its features, the route taken and, once scored, whether it succeeded: the rows the tabular head learns from, the experiment's counts and what a compiler reads. Calls to the fast and slow models are counted apart. **A row's label is the outcome, never the decider's own choice** (else the head learns to copy the model, and a confident skip is confirmed by its own rows): the task's success, the scorer's verdict, and the owner's corrections as the strongest rows; a small sample of calls is still sent to the model when the head is confident (a shadow check, to see drift). **Feedback is long-horizon:** each decision keeps a stable id, and an outcome that arrives later (the owner corrects it an hour on, a reminder is never acknowledged, a task is redone, a note is found wrong) attaches to that id as a signal, so a row's label can change long after the call. The log keeps the raw request and its outcome, not only features, so features can be recomputed when the embedder or the PCA changes. The log holds the owner's words verbatim: it needs a retention rule and a line in docs/privacy.md before real use. (e) **Dogfooding:** the owner will use the assistant (voice head, fast and slow models, worker, gate) as their own personal assistant with real tools and tasks; real use supplies the recurring task families and the labels for M-17's first experiment, beside the scripted harness. Which real tools come first is the owner's (privacy, docs/privacy.md). | decided |
| S-179 | **The gate's decider: TabICL over embeddings, with the fast model's probabilities as the cold start and the tie-break; vela3-fast kept as the catalog's alternative** (2026-11-22; research skill on S-178's three slots; the kernel's MCP tools are not loaded in this session, so the evidence is the trial and this row, to be written as claims from a session that has them). **Trial:** `scripts/trials/decider/` (`run.sh`; output `.state/trials/decider/summary.txt`): 96 utterances over the voice head's eight routes (talk, lookup, calculate, save_note, read_notes, remind, search, delegate; `cases.json`, **a dev set written by Claude, not held out from the author**); stage 1 in the voice head's container through the gateway, stage 2 in a trial image (python 3.13.17-slim-bookworm, torch 2.15.0 CPU, transformers 5.17.0, gliner2 2.1.0, tabicl 2.2.0, scikit-learn 1.9.1, all on PyPI before 2026-11-08), all on this machine's CPU. **Results** (accuracy, top-label ECE, Brier, median per decision): `fast` (LFM3 1.7B, eight described options, one letter, thinking off, the answer prefilled, probabilities from `top_logprobs`) 93/96, 0.058, 0.062, 296 ms; `vote` (S-170's, qwen3-embedding 0.6b, k=3, leave one out) 84/96, 0.049, 0.167, 52 ms; **`tabicl` (TabICLv2 checkpoint `tabicl-classifier-v2-20260212`, HF `jingang/TabICL` at 4dcd344, BSD-3-Clause; the embedding cut to 32 by PCA; 4-fold, the other folds as context) 90/96, 0.027 (the best), 0.104, 74 ms with the embedding (TabICL's share measured batched over a fold; one call alone not yet measured)**; `tabicl+fast` (the fast model's eight probabilities as columns) 94/96, 0.035, 0.048, 373 ms; `vela` (vllm-sr/vela3-fast at fa60e3a, Apache-2.0, 2026-10-31, zero shot, the System One request as is, network off) 85/96, 0.081, 0.157, 179 ms, 3.7 GB RSS; `gliner` (fastino/GLiNER2.5-Decide at 9d1bfb8, Apache-2.0, zero shot with label descriptions) 65/96, 0.323, 0.646, 288 ms, 4.3 GB, nearly every talk case called lookup, and its API gave only the top label's confidence (the description form may be misused; not pursued). **Online learning, TabICL's curve** (examples per route in its context, 5 draws): 1: 0.675, 2: 0.838, 4: 0.884, 8: 0.912. **Checked:** Bifrost v2.3.2 passes `logprobs`/`top_logprobs` through to llama.cpp b12627 and back; `reasoning_effort: none` plus an assistant prefill `Answer:` gives the letter as the first token (40-90 ms for three options). **Pick:** the gate asks TabICL over the embedding first (cheap, best calibrated, learns from each logged and scored route with no training); below its threshold, or for a route with too few examples, it adds the fast model's probabilities (a new route needs only its description); above the threshold it skips. **Why not the others:** vela3-fast is the best zero-shot small model here but needs 3.7 GB beside what runs and loses to the fast model already loaded; GLiNER as measured; the embedding vote is beaten on accuracy and Brier; Julia-1 (144M, Apache-2.0) was modified 2026-11-09 (under two weeks) and not tried; d1 is "other" licence (Liquid's; the owner's call, like LFM3). **Searched:** `github.com/andyrewlee/awesome-system-one` (read 2026-11-22), Hugging Face's model API for licence, dates and size, PyPI for packages. **Not yet:** mid-task calls (after a tool result: answer or which tool next), the S-170 held-out set, one decision's latency alone, the GPU, and held-out cases written by the owner from real use (dogfooding, S-178 e). **Likely optimistic (said to the owner):** the cases and the route descriptions were written together by the same author, so the descriptions fit the cases' wording (most help to `fast` and `vela`); the cases are clean, balanced and single-intent; the 4-fold split leaves near-twin templates on both sides (TabICL's score and curve flattered); 96 cases give overlapping 95% ranges for the top three (about 0.91-0.99, 0.87-0.97, 0.93-0.99). Each decider was run once with its first prompt (no selection over variants); GLiNER's likely misuse was not fixed. **Next:** freeze the deciders, then a blind set (the owner's own requests, and one written by another model from the route names alone, unread before the run), realistic route mix, Parakeet-transcribed speech, context-dependent and mid-task cases, a split by template, intervals reported. **To check before relying on it:** TabICLv2's limit on the number of classes; a cap on its context (recent plus balanced per route, or nearest by embedding); the fast model's letter-order bias (shuffle and average). | trial, pending a blind run |
| S-180 | **The gate follows Soar: skills propose, the decider selects, impasses are typed, and choosing and compiling are two learning paths** (2026-11-22; the owner asked whether the design is close enough to Soar's chunking and whether it is a good System 1; builds on S-178, S-179). (a) **Mapping:** Soar's rules that propose operators are skills' **conditions** (when a skill applies), kept beside their triggers; selection by preferences tuned by Soar-RL is **the decider** (TabICL over outcome-labelled rows, S-179), which chooses among the proposed skills and the model rungs; the impasse is the decider abstaining; the substate is the model call (System 2); **chunking is the skill compiler**, not built yet. (b) **Impasse types are logged** from the decider's output at no cost: `none` (no skill proposed), `tie` (the top two close), `low` (one ahead but under the threshold); each is a different signal to the compiler and to M-17's measures. (c) **Two learning paths, kept apart:** choosing (statistical, online, in Substrate's gate) and compiling (a solved trace becomes a skill whose conditions are what the answer depended on, as chunking backtraces, so it generalises on what mattered rather than on wording and works after one case; the worker's `cited` ids are the start of that trace; in the M-17 experiment). The log keeps each answer's dependencies (the tool results and inputs it used) for the compiler. (d) **Evaluation is prequential:** each row is predicted before its label is seen, in arrival order, then added to the context; this is the test that matches online use and shows drift; intervals, not single scores. (e) **Exploration is Thompson sampling** over each skill's success rate (a Beta draw per decision): new or doubtful skills are checked by the model more often, proven ones rarely; this is the shadow check of S-178 (d) made principled. (f) **AutoML later, not now:** with about a hundred self-written rows a search over models finds what fits the author's wording; once real use has given a few thousand rows, candidates (AutoGluon's zoo, which includes TabPFN, TabICL, Mitra, boosted trees and kNN; Optuna for thresholds) compete prequentially, each picked with the research skill (licences unchecked). (g) **Not System 1 yet in full:** the gate chooses; acting without the model also needs filling a skill's arguments (span-marking decision models such as vela3 are candidates for that, S-179) and the compiler. | decided |
| S-181 | **One assistant: the capability levels are model rungs in one loop, not separate agents; MetaSoar's experiments fold into Substrate; plain role names** (2026-11-22; the owner: "too many named projects going ... and i also don't know why our voice agent has two aux agents other than 2 higher levels of capability it can defer to"; "go ahead and revise the docs"). (a) **Why there were two:** history, not design: the companion (S-136, Claude Code on the owner's login) came first and the voice head handed work to it; the worker (S-163, our loop on the self-hosted slow model) came later; S-170 moved the voice head's work to the worker and left the companion behind the worker's `ask_companion` (propose). (b) **Now:** behind the voice front end is **one assistant**: the worker's loop (S-173's one agent loop), whose model calls the gate routes by rung: a compiled skill (no model), `fast`, `slow`, and `cloud` (Claude through the gateway's pass-through route on the owner's login), the last always behind `propose` because it sends the owner's words off the machine (docs/privacy.md P3). One loop, one log, one page. The voice front end stays its own part (talking needs a fast turn-taker) and keeps its quick tools (S-171). **The companion as a separate agent retires once the cloud rung works;** a Claude Code session remains an agent instance like any other harness (S-173), for what it is uniquely good at, such as coding in a repository. Its budget and meter (S-144) carry over to the cloud rung. (c) **Projects:** two products: **the Kernel** (the store; its own database and other users) and **Substrate** (everything that runs). **MetaSoar's experiments move into Substrate** (`experiments/`, beginning with M-17's), as envkit did (S-161); MetaSoar keeps the hypothesis and its write-up, and its repository is changed there, by its own commits. **Morphogen** stays out of the build (rule 9). **No "personal distribution" name:** the assistant is what the owner runs. (d) **Names inside Substrate are roles:** the assistant, the voice front end, the gate, the judge, the kernel; model aliases by role (`fast`, `slow`, `cloud`), never by model, as S-169 began. "Companion" and "worker" stay as the code's names until the merge, then go. (e) **Build order** (`docs/plan.md` step 3): the decision log; the proxy (OpenAI format first, passing through and logging); the deciders behind a threshold with impasse types and Thompson sampling (S-180); the voice front end and the assistant through it; the cloud rung and the Anthropic format, retiring the companion; the frozen blind run (S-179). (f) **The owner's to decide:** the gate's log keeps the owner's requests verbatim (S-178 d), which docs/privacy.md P6 (no prompts kept by default) forbids for a personal stream unless the owner turns transcripts on for it; the alternative is keeping only features (the embedding and the outcome), which loses recomputing features when the embedder changes. Recommended: turn transcripts on for the gate's log only, with P7's retention set. | decided |
| S-182 | **Experience, not a decision log; the owner's privacy terms for it; the assistant's first real tools** (2026-11-22; the owner: "is it really 'decision logs'? i think of my own system 1 as distilling responses to scenarios"; "ok to keep, there can be privacy settings including encryption and deletion"; "real tools should be files and search and kernel/substrate, along with an MCP for searching/installing MCPs from a trusted website"). Supersedes the name "decision log" in S-178 (d), S-180 and S-181 (e) and the P6 question of S-181 (f). (a) **Experience:** what the gate stores is the assistant's experience, one **episode** per model call: the **scenario** (the request, its context and features), the **response** (who answered: a skill, a rung, and what it said or did, with what it depended on), the **outcome** (labels that arrive later by the episode's id). Table `env.experience` (Soar's episodic memory). **System 1 is distilled from experience** in two forms: **skills** (a compiled response with conditions, chunking, one case enough) and **preferences** (which response fits which scenario, TabICL over episodes, Soar-RL's role). The gate is System 1 answering: it recalls a distilled response for the scenario, or hands the call to System 2 (the model) when none fits. (b) **Privacy (a loosening of docs/privacy.md P6 for this store only, on the owner's word):** episodes keep the owner's requests verbatim, with: encryption at rest of the text (key from `.env` through the `sec_*` verbs; pgcrypto or the volume, to be picked), deletion of one episode, a time range or all (P9; the embedding and every derived row go with it, since embeddings can be partly inverted to text), and a retention period the owner sets (P7). (c) **The assistant's first real tools:** **files** (the workspace now; folders the owner names, P1, read-only unless named writable); **search** (the SearXNG and Wikipedia tools the voice front end already has, S-171); **the kernel** (the `memory` profile it has, and the `substrate` profile's search and read); **Substrate itself** (status, the environment's waiting proposals and gates, logs and the catalog, read-only first; changes through `propose`); and **an MCP that finds and installs MCP servers from one trusted registry**: finding is free; **installing waits for the owner** (`propose`), pins the exact version, checks the licence (rule 7), runs the server in its own container (rule 6), and writes it into the catalog as a component (S-176) before the assistant may call it. Which registry (the official MCP Registry is the lead candidate) is picked with the research skill. | decided |
| S-183 | **Experience built: `env.experience` and `env.outcome`, the text encrypted with pgcrypto under a key the database never stores** (2026-11-22; plan step 3 (a); S-182). **Built:** `environment/sql/008_experience.sql` (creates pgcrypto 1.3 and pgvector 0.8.7 where missing; both ship in our Postgres image) and `environment/experience.py` (`Experience`: record, outcome, episodes, and the owner's text, forget, expire). An episode: `ref` (a uuid outcomes and callers refer to), caller, login, the request and response as `pgp_sym_encrypt` bytea, features, embedder and embedding (`vector`, no fixed size, so the embedder can change), responder (`skill:<name>`, `fast`, `slow`, `cloud`, or `model:<alias>`), what it depended on, the decider's output, the impasse (`none`, `tie`, `low`, or null). Outcomes: `ok` (boolean), `correct` (a responder), `score` (0..1), each with its source and an encrypted note; many per episode, the latest of each kind is the label. **Kept whole:** a trigger refuses any change or delete except inside the owner's forget; outcomes cascade with their episode. **Who:** new role `env_gate` (record, add outcomes, the decider's context `env.episodes(...)` with no text); `env_agent` may add outcomes only; reading the text (`env.episode_text`), forgetting (`env.forget_experience`: one, a time range, all; a vague call is refused) and expiring (`env.expire_experience`, from the setting `experience.retention_days`, none set means keep) are granted to no login. **Encryption choice:** pgcrypto rather than an encrypted volume or client-side encryption: no new package, the key is passed as a bound parameter, and Postgres here logs no statements (`log_statement=none`) and no parameters on error (`log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0`, read 2026-11-22); the embeddings are not encrypted (the decider reads them in the database), so deleting removes them with the text. **Key:** `EXPERIENCE_KEY` (`deploy/secrets.txt`, token:32, made by `scripts/settings.sh`; losing it loses the stored text). **Retention** is not scheduled yet: pg_cron is not installed in database `substrate`; the gate calls `expire` (S-183 leaves that to the gate's build). **Tested:** `scripts/tests/test_experience.py`, 29 checks (encryption, a wrong key, the gate unable to read text, select, change or forget, the assistant only adding outcomes, labels by latest, constraints, forgetting one, a range and all, expiry by the setting); all eleven suites pass; migrated on this machine's database. | adopted |
| S-184 | **OpenEnv is adopted fully, not wrapped: Substrate's environments are OpenEnv environments, in production and simulation** (2026-11-23; the owner: "let's not overlook openenv, that might save us from recreating its features"; "solidify whether we want to actually try to integrate fully with open env or if we're going to be re-implementing its features just to wrap it which seems unnecessary"; read `huggingface.co/docs/openenv` guides simulation-vs-production, reference core, tutorials mcp-environment and claude-code-harness, 2026-11-23). **What OpenEnv gives and we use as it is:** the environment classes (`Environment`, `MCPEnvironment`), the server (`create_app`, `mode="production"` or simulation), the clients (`EnvClient`, `MCPToolClient`), simulation episodes (`reset`, `step`, `state`, rewards, `done`) and rubrics (`Rubric`, `Gate`, `WeightedSum`, `LLMJudge`), production mode (the same class serving its MCP tools at `/mcp`, no episodes), the harness adapter (RFC 005 `HarnessEnvironment`: Claude Code headless, its own tools off, only the environment's MCP tools; `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` redirects its model calls), Docker packaging and Hugging Face distribution (`from_docker_image`, `from_env(repo_id)`), and Harbor's training traces later. **What it lacks, built inside our environments rather than around them:** persistence (state in Postgres, so many sessions share one live environment), auth (the database's logins; the server itself has none and its docs say keep it on localhost or behind your own auth), approval gates (a `propose` tool returns "waiting for approval", or MCP's `input_required` if the 2026-07-28 revision is final: to check), events to subscribers, and recording model calls. Simulation `reset` clones a template database. **Package (PyPI, read 2026-11-23):** `openenv`, BSD-3-Clause, Python 3.10 or newer, dependencies fastapi, pydantic, uvicorn, huggingface_hub, openai, websockets and a few small ones; 0.2.0 on 2026-06-02, weekly since, 0.14.0 on 2026-11-19; **pin 0.12.0 (2026-11-05)**, the newest at least two weeks old; expect breaking changes and keep our code against it thin. Not `openenv-core` (an older separate package, 0.3.0 of 2026-05-11). RFC 003 (MCP as the action boundary) and RFC 005 (harnesses) are still in review; the docs disagree on which routes production mode serves. **The spike before building on it:** (1) many agents' sessions share one Postgres-backed live environment; (2) events reach a subscriber in production mode; (3) `reset` clones a template database fast enough; (4) Claude Code runs through the harness adapter; (5) it runs against our model proxy through `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, with an episode id carried in a header; (6) a skill's answer reaches Claude Code in a form its loop accepts (final text or a well-formed tool-use block); (7) a rubric's score attaches afterwards to every model call of its episode. **MetaSoar** moves its worlds (envkit's Mario, ARC-AGI-3, the stream) and M-17's experiment onto OpenEnv; envkit retires into that. | decided; spike pending |
| S-185 | **Substrate's shape: a top-level orchestrator that maps, orchestrates and optimises a working system, on a Postgres kernel; every dependency is a contract, our projects are default fillers** (2026-11-23; the owner: "a top-down hierarchy where the top-level is observing a working system and keeping it working by adjusting and orchestrating, everything is handled by sub-agents potentially within shared environments"; "the orchestrator being a regular LLM harness with skills and MCP servers and hooks"; "like it's the GNU operating system running on top of a Linux kernel but postgres has been turned into our kernel"; "the core of substrate would be to map and orchestrate and optimize"; "help me figure out where we draw the lines"). **The layers:** the **Kernel** (Postgres made into a kernel: knowledge with provenance, objects with properties, gated actions and events, logins, the record; data transformations and what happens at the Postgres level); **Substrate** (the orchestrator kit: the skills `map`, `catalog`, `research`, `orchestrate`, `optimize`, plus `setup` and `upstream`; MCP servers; hooks that enforce gates inside the harness; observing by subscription; the adapters (OpenEnv, the model proxy, compose decoding); the reference stack; and the document of how it fits; only a little agent framing, no LLM or harness methodology); **Morphogen** (the optimiser, a sub-agent pluggable into anyone's system: cells, optimizers behind a judge the optimizer cannot edit, the still-works gate, the system guard, the evidence log); **distributions** (what is built with it: kinds of environments, multi-stage agents, the assistant, the SMB Foundry or AI ERP; **folders in this repository, `distributions/`, until Substrate's shape is settled**); **MetaSoar** (the research on cognitive architecture). **Dependencies are contracts only, no cycles:** the Kernel needs only Postgres; Morphogen needs cell files and reads Backstage entities when present; Substrate needs OpenEnv and MCP (environments and delegation), Backstage entity files (the map), Morphogen's cell contract (the optimiser), and an OpenAI- or Anthropic-compatible model endpoint; distributions need Substrate and the fillers they choose. The test that the lines are clean: Substrate pointed at a folder of catalog files and one OpenEnv environment still runs. **Slots and default fillers:** environment and delegation: OpenEnv + MCP, with A2A v1.0's task states for a delegated task's lifecycle; three kinds of filler: an OpenEnv environment (rehearsal, scoring, packaging), a harness sub-agent (Claude Code's own sub-agents for quick work inside the orchestrator's session; a separate harness process, with its own Kernel login, context and record, for long or risky work), and custom (any MCP server: a service, a script, a non-LLM agent, the Kernel's Postgres environment); the map: Backstage entity files, read and written over MCP, the Kernel by default; the optimiser: Morphogen; the harness: Claude Code; models: Bifrost. **Autopoiesis:** Substrate's own stack is an environment like any other (its compose file into the map and back, S-177), so the orchestrator maintains and grows its own system with the same skills, imposing no functionality downstream. **Morphogen as the optimiser (rule 9 was misread: it was taken out of the build only to iterate on it quickly):** GitHub's main (rebuild 7, 2026-11-21) is already a Claude Code plugin (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json` v0.8.0, Apache-2.0) with an MCP server (`status`, `structure_check`, `structure_scope`, `structure_render`, `measure`, `probe`, `judge`, `judge_change`, `tune`, `optimizers_list`, `evidence_query`), the skills `morphogen` and `map`, the agents `worker` and `reviewer`, an optimizer catalog (grid, random, hill-climb, annealing, Optuna, GEPA, a bandit `selector`) with an entry-point group `morphogen.optimizers`, and reads Backstage; so it slots in by installing the plugin in the orchestrator's harness. Underneath them all, npm and PyPI: trust per package (publisher, attestations, deps.dev). **The installer's policy:** search the official registry (and Docker's catalog); prefer a Docker-catalog image, else a namespace-verified publisher, with GitHub's curation as a signal; every install still goes through `propose`, an exact pin, the licence check, its own container and a catalog entry (S-182 c); listing never implies trust; a server whose tools ask for broad credentials is flagged in the proposal. (b) **Licences of downloads (rule 7 extended):** rule 7 governs what the repository contains, links or bundles; a model or tool the user downloads themselves may be recommended whatever its licence, with the licence named. So **LFM3 1.7B is the recommended fast model** (`.env.example` says so; `LLM_FAST_MODEL` stays empty, since the file is the user's download); its licence, LiquidAI's LFM Open License v1.0, is named and still to be read. Amends S-185's slot table (`docs/framework.md`, "Slots" and "Optimise"). (a) **Morphogen** (its cell contract, S-193) changes what a cell's `change` names, files and settings, and its judge scores a candidate by the cell's tests and measure. (b) **An OpenEnv environment** (S-184, S-192) scores a model's or a policy's episodes by its rubrics; a trainer (reinforcement learning, distillation) learns from those scores, and the result (weights, a compiled skill, a router) is judged on held-out episodes before it is used. S-192's point 7 (a rubric's score attached to every model call of its episode) is what makes this possible. (c) **One rule over both:** whatever is changed cannot edit what judges it (a cell's tests and scorer, an environment's rubric and held-out episodes), and a winner is a proposal the person approves. (d) **The `optimize` skill** (plan step 5) picks which one for a target: a change to text or configuration goes to Morphogen; a change to how a model behaves on a task family goes to an environment and a trainer. (c) **MCP is the common interface:** each module serves its tools, an MCP router (picked with the `research` skill) puts them behind one endpoint for agents and the desktop, checks tokens and applies the gates (a `changes_world` tool through `propose`, into one review lane); OpenEnv environments join the same way. (d) **The layers:** kernel core (schema, module contract, `kk`, the router, the validator, the shell with Settings); kernel modules (a default set and families, the environment among them); Substrate (its modules from its repository, its peripherals, the orchestrator kit); distributions (configuration only). Kernel decisions follow there for its part (`kk serve` split into modules, the router, `kernel-mcp`'s profiles as modules' tools). | S-204 | **The MCP router, research step 3: agentgateway passes every case that rests on the router; Bifrost has an MCP gateway but not our tokens; the kernel's tokens need to change for either** (2026-11-23; S-203's router; the `research` skill; the owner: "doesn't bifrost have an MCP router?"). The kernel's MCP tools are not wired into this session (the handoff's loose end 6), so the need, candidates and results are here and in `scripts/trials/mcp-router/`, `.state/trials/mcp-router/`, to be written as claims later. **The need:** one streamable-HTTP MCP endpoint in front of stdio and HTTP servers; the caller's kernel token checked and its identity passed on; per-tool rules; a call-out where a person's approval can hold a call; a permissive licence, a release two weeks old, one container; MCP 2025-11-25. **Candidates** (two Sonnet subagents: GitHub search for "mcp gateway", "mcp proxy" and "mcp aggregator" and the awesome-mcp-servers "Aggregators" list; then each lead checked in its repository's docs, schemas and releases): agentgateway (Linux Foundation, Apache-2.0, Rust, v1.7.0 of 2026-10-27); Bifrost (Apache-2.0, ours already, v2.3.2 of 2026-10-20: an MCP gateway in the open edition, stdio, HTTP and SSE servers behind `/mcp`, tool filters per server, key and request, `PreMCPHook` and `PostMCPHook` in Go or WASM plugins; but callers are its own virtual keys or the OAuth tokens it issues, identity passed on only by its enterprise `token_exchange`, and stdio servers need a custom image); IBM ContextForge (Apache-2.0, v1.1.0: HS256, `tool_pre_invoke` plugins, identity propagation; Postgres and Redis besides, protocol default 2025-06-18); mcphub, tbxark/mcp-proxy, mcpproxy-go (static tokens or keys, no call-level hook); ruled out: MetaMCP (archived), microsoft/mcp-gateway (Kubernetes only, too new), docker/mcp-gateway (Docker socket, rule 5), five AGPL and two GPL projects. **agentgateway trial** (`run.sh agentgateway`: its binary on Python 3.13.17 slim trixie, since its image is distroless and the binary needs glibc 2.39; a stdio tools server with a read tool and a world-changing one; the kernel's MCP over HTTP; a decision service standing in for the review lane): 11 of 13 cases pass: refuses no token and a foreign key; takes a token with a `role` claim; initialize at 2025-11-25; both backends' tools as one list, prefixed by target (`tools_echo`, `kernel_recall`); the read tool passes; the world-changing tool is held until a person approves, then goes; the decision service is asked per tool call with the caller's role and the tool (`extAuthz` on `has(mcp.tool)`, then `authorization` requiring its decision); a CEL rule on the token's claims limits an agent's token to the read tool. The two failures are the kernel's: it cannot verify the trial's token, so `whoami` through the router fails and its tool list is the anonymous profile's. **Found:** agentgateway takes only asymmetric keys (RSA, EC, Ed25519; an HS256 `oct` key is refused) and requires an issuer and a key id, so the kernel's tokens (`kk/cli.py` `mint`: HS256, no `iss`, `aud` or `kid`) would be signed with an Ed25519 key, with its public key published as a JWKS; that is better practice (a verifier cannot mint) and a kernel decision. agentgateway sends the backend's Host without its port, so the kernel's `KK_MCP_ALLOWED_HOSTS` gains `kernel` (`deploy/compose.yaml`). **A secret leaked in the trial:** agentgateway's error for the refused `oct` key printed the key, which was the kernel's `KK_JWT_SECRET`, into this session's transcript; the runner now writes router logs to a file only (mode 600), and rotating the secret is the owner's to approve. **Pending:** the pick (recommended: agentgateway, with the kernel signing Ed25519; Bifrost would need a token shim of ours in front and a Go plugin for the gate). | trial |

Supersedes S-204's pending pick: agentgateway and Bifrost stay in the catalog (agentgateway in front for a deployment that needs its OAuth, rate limits or telemetry). (a) **The rules in the database** (`environment/sql/010_mcp.sql`, applied by `scripts/db.py init`): `env.mcp_server` and `env.mcp_tool` (name, schemas, annotations; never deleted, marked absent when no longer listed); each tool an actuator `mcp.tool_<id>` whose gate starts from its annotations (`readOnlyHint` true is `free`, anything else `propose`) and is then the person's (a connector registering again never changes it); `env.mcp_call` is `env.act`, so a call is an `env.action` recorded whole; `pg_notify('env_action')` on every status change; logins `mcp_router` (role `env_router`: list, call for an authenticated caller, read outcomes and the record, record a person's decision) and `mcp_connector` (role `env_connector`: register, read its queues, report done), passwords `MCP_ROUTER_DB_PASSWORD`, `MCP_CONNECTOR_DB_PASSWORD` (`deploy/secrets.txt`). (b) **The router** (`mcp-router/`, Go 1.25.14 by digest, go-sdk v1.8.0, pgx v5.11.0, HS256 by hand with the standard library; built by a Sonnet subagent to a written contract, reviewed and run here): `serve` is MCP over streamable HTTP, stateless, the server built per request from `env.mcp_tools()` so a new tool shows at once; the kernel's token as it is (HS256, `role`), the caller's role passed to `env.mcp_call`; a call waits for its outcome on one LISTEN connection fanned out to waiters (default 15 s), then answers with the stored result, a refusal, or "action N is proposed: it waits for a person's approval; ask router_outcome"; a built-in `router_outcome`; a JSON API (tools, calls, decide, login with the person's kernel login) and an embedded page (Waiting with Approve and Reject, Calls, Tools; its own CSP, framable by the desktop only). `connect` reaches one server (stdio or streamable HTTP), registers it every 60 s, applies the calls on its queues and reports `env.done`; for an HTTP server it signs each call as its caller (a 5-minute token for the caller's role), so the kernel still decides what that caller may do. (c) **The stack:** compose services `mcp-router` and `mcp-kernel` (the kernel's MCP; listing as `person_owner`), image `ghcr.io/openreplicant/substrate-mcp-router` (published by `images.yml`, built when not); Caddy serves it at 127.0.0.1:8083; the desktop's apps (`deploy/caddy/apps.json`) and the shell's `frame-src` include it; `check.sh` checks it; the catalog has `component:mcp-router`, `component:mcp-kernel`, `api:mcp-router`; `scripts/test.sh mcp-router` runs its Go tests (vet and test, in the Containerfile's Go image). (d) **Checked:** `scripts/trials/mcp-router/run.sh ours`, 15/15: refuses no token and a foreign one; takes the kernel's token as it is; initialize at 2025-11-25; both servers' tools as one list (`tools_*`, 20 `kernel_*`, `router_outcome`); a read tool at once; the kernel answering as the caller; an agent's world-changing call held; an agent cannot decide; a person cannot approve their own call; the held call outlives a router restart and is approved through the page's API; applied by its connector with the result kept; every call in the record with tool, caller, gate, status and who decided; a read call's round trip through the queue 9 ms. `page.sh`, 7/7 in a browser: the page's CSP, the person's sign-in, a waiting call with its tool and caller, approved on the page and applied, the record and tools, no page errors or CSP violations. The desktop trial (S-200) still 23/23 with three apps; `check.sh` and all suites pass. (e) **Found:** the kernel's MCP tools carry no annotations, so all 20 start behind `propose` (the safe default); the trial opened `kernel_recall` as a person would (`scripts/env.py gate`); the kernel's read tools should say `readOnlyHint` (a small kernel pull request). A connector retries a server that is not up yet with a growing wait (here 30 s after the kernel's start). (f) **Noted for the catalog** (the owner): pg_net (Supabase, Apache-2.0) as a dispatcher from inside a transaction or trigger, for webhooks and plain HTTP, beside the queue for sessions and stateful work; it would go into the Postgres image. (g) **Where it lives:** in Substrate, beside the environment it rests on, as its own module with its own image; S-203 places the router in the kernel's core, so moving it there is the owner's call once it settles. | done |

| S-209 | **The re-cut: mapping, research, cataloguing and optimising are one file-based kit, in Morphogen; the kernel is the layer above it; Substrate is what runs on both** (2026-11-24; the owner, asked what Substrate is and how little it can depend on: "the thing that I wanted to make substrate or at least some project dedicated to was the idea of building a map and maintaining it along with optimization targets"; "maybe with just skills and interfaces that could be easily exchanged"; "at first all that was going under morphogen, and maybe that makes sense"; "none of this needs the kernel especially since the kernel supports file ingestion ... if we need the database for indexing search and traversal it's only a matter of ingesting those files into kernel"; then "Yes that's right and I feel good about it finally"). Supersedes S-185's layer table and S-190's placement where they differ. (a) **The kit, in Morphogen:** packaged on the harness the user chooses (a Claude Code plugin first), it does four things on files only, with no database: **map** (interview a person, do hypothetical design work, find facts through whatever systems the harness reaches, and build the map out and detail substructures iteratively), **research and catalog** (search openly for the options for each role and document each one: its interfaces and adjustable parameters, or at least a link to its docs once it is chosen), **optimise** (Morphogen's cells and its judge, unchanged), and **orchestrate** (nothing to build: the harness does it, given MCP servers for access). The map is Backstage entity files; the targets are Morphogen's cell file. (b) **Why Morphogen:** it is already a plugin with an MCP server, has a `map` skill and the cell format, and reads Backstage; two homes for mapping is the fragmentation the owner avoids; the judge's independence from what an optimiser edits (Morphogen's reason to stand alone) is untouched by adding mapping and cataloguing beside it. Substrate's `map`, `catalog` and `research` skills move there, rewritten for files instead of the kernel's tools, by pull request (rule 9, the `upstream` skill); `docs/morphogen-kit.md` is the proposal. (c) **The kernel is the layer above, not below:** its `encoder-backstage` ingests the same files when indexing, search or traversal is needed; the kit never requires it. (d) **Substrate** is a base implementation of what can be built on the kernel and the kit: the live environment and its gates, the MCP router, the channel, the desktop, the assistant; it uses Morphogen's plugin like any user. (e) **"Skill" means an Agent Skill** and nothing else; we do not name our own things skills. (f) **System 1 research** (learned policies shipped in a skill folder as scripts with triggers and a swappable matcher from state to policy: text and a model's probabilities first, then embeddings or tabular models; the language model falls back to the skill's instructions when nothing matches, and the case is recorded) is MetaSoar's, after this; it ships as a convention for a skill folder, so the kit can use it without depending on it. | proposed: the Morphogen PR is the owner's to review |

Two uses: **the engagement** (the consultant runs the kit with the client: interviews, documents, the systems they grant access to) and **prospecting** (the same mapping run on public sources about a firm, producing a first opportunity list before the first call, which could be automated). | S-211 | **Substrate is a default configuration, not a product: a harness set up to use the kernel and Morphogen to get things done, interactively; everything else is a kernel module or a standard module type; work goes to Morphogen and the kernel first** (2026-11-24; asked whether Substrate is "basically a harness with morphogen installed, talking to the kernel, with openenv integrations and a default stack including bifrost and mCP servers", the owner: "I'm feeling less like working on substrate at the moment then just making sure everything we'd be doing with it actually works in morphogen and kernel"; "Claude gives us the benefit of being able to use an anthropic subscription and we can certainly customize the harness configuration so that it works for what we want which is the whole point here of providing a default configuration which uses kernel and morphogen to get shit done and is interactive"; "substrate doesn't need to add its own things like the desktop UI that can actually sit in the kernel as a module and everything becomes either an existing standard module type or a knowledge kernel module"). Supersedes S-209 (d) and S-185's "Substrate" row. (a) **What Substrate is:** a harness's configuration (Claude Code first, for the Anthropic subscription; OpenHands noted as a candidate if a long-running harness is needed): settings, hooks, MCP servers, Morphogen's plugin, the kernel's tools, and a default stack (the model gateway, local models), with the means to bring it up and check it. It adds no parts of its own. (b) **Every part is one of two things:** a **kernel module** (the kernel's module contract, S-203) or a **standard module type** (a compose service, an MCP server, an Agent Skill, a harness plugin). `docs/kernel-moves.md` lists each part of the tree and where it goes; the kernel's side changes by its own pull requests, which the owner handles. (c) **The bet**, kept as Substrate's test: an ordinary strong harness in a well-built environment is the agent we want; it holds where one implementation project runs end to end on a demo SMB (Morphogen maps it and picks an opportunity, the harness carries it out under the kernel's gates, a person approves what matters, Morphogen judges the result) with no new core code, and fails where that run needs some. (d) **Order:** Morphogen's kit (S-209) and the kernel's modules first; Substrate's configuration after, from what they need. | direction set |
| S-212 | **Substrate is the home of the kernel's optional modules and the default distribution; the kernel's core ships without them; agents and experiments build on the distribution** (2026-11-24; the owner: "the desktop UI and other things which could be Colonel modules but we would not bundle them with the core instead they would ship separately and that's where substrate could be the home of these things which are nice options to have but should not necessarily ship with the core and we'll just included in a default distribution and then we can build all our other agents or experiments on top of that distribution"). Amends S-211 (b) and (d): the parts S-211 sent into the kernel's repository stay here instead, written to the kernel's module contract (S-203) and shipped from this repository. (a) **The kernel's core** is what every kernel user needs and nothing more: knowledge, review, authentication, the module contract and the record. (b) **Substrate's modules**, optional, each a kernel module of its own: the live environment and its gates (its `propose` on the kernel's review lane), the channel into a harness, the MCP router, the desktop (the window manager, the launcher, framed apps) and the assistant's conversation. The desktop's window manager is in the kernel's shell today (the kernel's D-127): whether it moves out to a Substrate module is the kernel's decision, by its own pull request. (c) **The default distribution**, also here: the kernel's core, Substrate's modules, Morphogen's plugin, a harness configured to use them (Claude Code first), and the default stack (the model gateway, local models). (d) **Built on it:** our other agents and experiments (the assistant, MetaSoar's work, the games and embodied environments), each a distribution or a module on top of the default one. (e) **Order:** Morphogen's kit first; then Substrate's parts as kernel modules, in place; then the default distribution; then the test of the bet (S-211 c). `docs/kernel-moves.md` becomes `docs/parts.md`. | direction set |
| S-213 | **A part is a kernel module only when it needs the kernel; the desktop is built here, for use cases** (2026-11-24; the owner: "we're not thinking everything in substrate is going to be packaged as a knowledge kernel module it might be a skill or an mCP server or some default collection of external services"; "now we can build the desktop separately and for use cases rather than just the hypothetical that we would leave in Colonel and once it's solidified it could be made perfect for the core distribution of Colonel and only the substrate specific parts or other system parts layered on top"; "it might wind up being its own product too ... probably not where the money is"). Amends S-212 (b). (a) **The rule:** a part of Substrate is a kernel module only when it needs the kernel (its own role, its data in the kernel's database, the review lane); otherwise it is the standard type it already is: an Agent Skill, an MCP server, a compose service, or an external service in the default collection. So the environment and its gates are a kernel module; the channel and the router are MCP servers (the router a compose service whose registry is in the environment module); the gateway and local models are external services. (b) **The desktop** (a modular web UI that hosts anything, and that agents can drive) is built in this repository against real use cases, not left in the kernel as a hypothetical; once it settles it may go into the kernel's core distribution, leaving only Substrate's own parts layered on it here, or become a product of its own. It is not where the money is (S-210), so it is built only as far as the use cases need. | direction set |
| S-214 | **The desktop stays in the kernel; Substrate's own parts are installed onto its kernel instance** (2026-11-24; the owner: "maybe we should leave the 'desktop ui' where it is if its under the kernel repo, and we'll add substrate-specific parts in substrate's repo to be installed on its kernel instance. simpler, right?"). | S-218 | **The stack on kernel `e4ed8e2`; an MCP tool's gate follows its annotations until a person sets it; the desktop's host port is a setting** (2026-11-24; the owner, on port 8080 taken: "use any other available port, i'm working with 8080 right now"; on the gates: "Free, and change the rule"). (b) **The gate rule** (`environment/sql/012_mcp_gates.sql`, superseding the "a connector registering again never changes a gate" of `010_mcp.sql` and S-205): the kernel's tools were registered before #138, so its 14 read-only tools kept `propose` and every read waited for a person. Now each registration sets again a gate whose `set_by` is the registering connector (no person has set it since): from `env.mcp_policy` first, else readOnlyHint true is `free` and anything else `propose`, in both directions. | S-219 | **The environment is a kernel module: a proposed action waits in the kernel's review lane, and the decision made there is the action's** (2026-11-27; `docs/parts.md` "Order" step 2, first part; S-212, S-213 (a), S-214: a part is a kernel module when it needs the kernel's database, a role and the review lane). (a) **Through the kernel's module contract** (its `kernel/schemas/module.schema.json`, `modules/_template`, `kk module install`, read at kernel `e4ed8e2`): `environment/module.yaml` (`urn:kk:module:environment`, kind `control`, slots `trigger` and `actuator`, requires `review-lane`, role `kkm_environment`, runtime a python-service) and `environment/migration.sql` (the role, its agent row, the role granted to the kernel's login, as `actuator-compose` does). `scripts/kernel.sh init` runs `kk module install` on it after `kk init`, so the kernel checks the manifest and its licence and applies the migration once. The environment's own schema `env` stays migrated by `environment/db.py` (numbered files, each once): the kernel's contract applies a module's migration once and has no upgrade step, and `env` has seven files already; `013_review_lane.sql` gives the module's role `env.review_waiting()` and `env.decide` and nothing else in `env`. (b) **Its service** (`environment/review.py`; compose `environment`, in the kernel's image with `../environment` mounted, so it uses the kernel's `kk.lib` as every kernel module does: the kernel's login `kk_authenticator`, switched to `kkm_environment`): each waiting action becomes one change set in the review lane, keyed `environment:action:<id>`, in scope `urn:kk:scope:default`: `urn:kk:environment:action:<id>` a `sosa:Actuation`, `sosa:madeByActuator` its actuator, `as:actor` the caller's kernel agent when it is one, an `rdfs:label` (an MCP tool by the name its caller used, the caller, the action's id) and `dcterms:description` its input in full; standard terms from the kernel's packs, no new vocabulary. The kernel validates it and holds it for a reviewer, so the person decides it in the desktop's Review with everything else. Approved, the kernel applies it (the approved request is kept as claims, five per action) and the service calls `env.decide(approve, the reviewer, the note)`: queued for its actuator; rejected, refused with the reviewer's note. The kernel refuses a change set's approval by its submitter (the module) and the environment a proposal's by its caller, so a person who approves a request they made has it refused, with the reason. It wakes on `env_action` and `kernel_change_set` and looks every 5 s (`KK_SWEEP_SECONDS`), because a rejection sends no notification. (c) **One place to decide:** the router decides nothing any more (its `/api/decide` and the page's Approve and Reject buttons are gone; its Waiting list says to decide in Review; `env.decide` is revoked from `env_router`), and `scripts/env.py decide` is gone. (d) **Checked:** `scripts/tests/test_review.py`, 19/19 in a throwaway kernel (the install and its repeat; one change set per waiting action, held for review, saying what, by whom, with the input; approval, rejection with a note, self-approval refused; the router may not decide; the module may decide and may not act or set a gate); every suite passes (environment 44, review 19, kernel 27, UI 36, gateway 15, catalog 9, the router's Go tests). End to end on a throwaway stack (compose project `recut`, its own ports and volumes): an agent's `kernel_remember` through the router waited, appeared in the kernel's `review_queue` for `person_owner`, was approved with the kernel's own `review` tool and carried out by the kernel's connector; a second was rejected and the router's `router_outcome` said refused, by `person_owner`, "not now". (e) **Found, for the kernel:** `kk module install` gives a python-service module's role a login with a new random password on every run, even when the module is installed (unused here: the service switches from the kernel's login); there is no upgrade path for a module's migration; a rejection sends no `kernel_change_set` notification. Each is a small kernel pull request when wanted, none needed now. (f) **Privacy:** an approved request's input is now a claim in the default scope, which every agent with default clearance can read; before, it was only in `env.action`. Whether proposals go to a scope of their own is the owner's call (`docs/privacy.md`). | adopted |
| S-220 | **The channel and the MCP router are MCP servers on the environment module; the channel connects as its own login** (2026-11-27; `docs/parts.md` "Order" step 2, second part). (a) **The channel** (`environment/channel.py`, stdio, started by the harness) was already an MCP server reading the environment's queues, but it connected as Postgres's superuser. It now connects with `DATABASE_URL`, its own login `mcp_channel` (role `env_agent`: publish, act through the gates, read and acknowledge the queues it subscribed; it cannot decide, set a gate or read another's queue), password `MCP_CHANNEL_DB_PASSWORD` (`deploy/secrets.txt`, made by `scripts/settings.sh`, the login by `scripts/db.py init`). `scripts/assistant.sh` writes it into the channel's MCP entry (`.state/assistant/mcp.json`, mode 600, beside the token it already held); `013_review_lane.sql` gives the login the assistant's queues made before. Without `DATABASE_URL` it still falls back to `ENV_URL`, `STORE_URL`, then the superuser, for a person at the command line. It stays in the `environment` package: it is a few lines over the module's functions, not a part with code of its own. (b) **The router** (`mcp-router/`, Go) is already a compose-service MCP server whose registry (`env.mcp_server`, `env.mcp_tool`, `env.mcp_policy`) and gates are the environment module's schema; it now decides nothing (S-219 c). S-205 (g)'s question, whether it moves into the kernel's core, stays open. (c) **The catalog:** `component:environment` is now a service with its compose service, `component:channel` is new. (d) **Checked:** four new checks in `scripts/tests/test_environment.py` (the channel's session runs on an `env_agent` login; that login may not decide, set a gate or read a queue it did not subscribe), and the channel started from `assistant.sh`'s dry-run settings on the throwaway stack initialised as `mcp_channel` and acted through the gates. | adopted |

The Assistant view (`desktop/view-assistant/`, a view module in the kernel's contract, mounted into the kernel's container, S-206) and its messages as signals in `env` (`011_desktop.sql`) are the target shape already; its server `desktop/server.py` is an MCP server through the router, as S-213 (a) wants. What it lacks (a chat library picked with the `research` skill, S-208) waits for the next use of the assistant. `ui/` is not retired: the kernel's Map view draws one map, and Substrate's many maps (S-207: a map as an item, one marked self) are not in the kernel yet. | adopted |
| S-222 | **A preview release, v0.1.0-preview.1: images pinned to the release, a fresh install tested, a second copy on one machine possible** (2026-11-27; the owner wants projects released, Morphogen first, Substrate as a clearly labelled preview while the re-cut is under way; numbered after the re-cut's S-219 to S-221, PR #32). (a) **Versions:** `VERSION` holds the release (`0.1.0-preview.1`); `deploy/compose.yaml`'s image defaults, `image_ref` in `scripts/lib.sh` (`SUBSTRATE_VERSION`, `KERNEL_VERSION`) and `.env.example` name it and the kernel release it pins (`0.1.0`, the kernel's tag on `e4ed8e2`'s code), no longer `main`; `scripts/tests/test_release.py` keeps them equal, checks the images workflow publishes `v*` tags (it already did: the tag as pushed and the version without the v) and that release notes exist (`docs/release-notes/`). A kernel image that does not pull is built from its tag `v<version>`; `scripts/build.sh` pulls or builds the router's image too. A working checkout that wants `main` sets the tags in `.env` (an existing `.env` keeps what it has). (b) **The fresh install** (a clone in a temporary folder, the README only, beside other stacks on the owner's machine): it failed three ways, each fixed: the desktop's framed-app ports 8081 to 8083 were fixed and taken on this machine (now `DESKTOP_GATEWAY_PORT`, `DESKTOP_MAPS_PORT`, `DESKTOP_ROUTER_PORT`: compose maps them to Caddy's fixed 8081 to 8083, the Caddyfile's frame-src names them, `apps.json` is rendered by Caddy's `templates` with `{{env}}`, `check.sh` and `assistant.sh` read them); a first `up.sh` stopped in `gateway.py prune` while Bifrost was still making its tables (prune now waits for none of them: with any missing there is nothing stale); and a second copy would have shared the first one's data and tools container (`COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME`, `TOOLS_CONTAINER`, `KERNEL_ARTIFACTS_VOLUME` are settings beside `DB_VOLUME` and `OLLAMA_VOLUME`; `tools.sh` refuses to replace another checkout's container, which it did before: the test removed the owner's stopped `substrate-tools`, which `scripts/tools.sh start` recreates). `bootstrap.sh` checks for curl (rule 6 names it). With those, bootstrap, up, check and every test suite passed on the fresh clone. (c) **The README** says what the preview is, what works, what is moving and what it needs; release notes are `docs/release-notes/v0.1.0-preview.1.md`. (d) **The owner releases** (not done here): the kernel's `v0.1.0` first, then this repository's `v0.1.0-preview.1`, each pushed tag publishing its images. | adopted — confirm |
| S-223 | **A manager agent: a router with a ledger, which knows every project, hands work to workers and never does it itself; the design is `docs/manager-agent.md`** (2026-11-27; the owner wants a main agent that acts like a project manager, aware of all projects, delegating to workers, staying responsive, with a UI in the kernel's desktop: "yes, start the manager agent design"). S-223 because #32 takes S-219 to S-221 and #33 S-222; builds on #32's design (the environment as a kernel module, `propose` in the kernel's review lane, the channel and router as MCP servers), not its code. (a) **The portfolio** in the environment module (`env.project`, `env.worker`, `env.decision`, a new `014_portfolio.sql`), written only through a portfolio MCP server behind the router that checks each caller's token, with a files-only mirror as Backstage entities (`portfolio/`, Morphogen's format, S-209). The manager cannot set a project done, set its class, write a worker's state or answer a decision; a worker reports only on itself; the supervisor cannot answer. (b) **The manager** is a Claude Code session on Haiku in its own container (as the companion, S-136, S-144) with no built-in tools (`--tools ''`, so no Bash, edits or Agent tool), only the channel and the router, a hook that caps a turn at 8 tool calls, and its calls metered at the gateway as `manager:session`. Haiku, not `fast` (S-170): the manager needs Claude Code's loop with about ten tools and writes the briefs workers succeed or fail by; its routing choices are recorded so they can be replayed against `fast`. (c) **Workers** start through actuators `pm.start` (free: an `own` project on the subscription or a quota) and `pm.start_gated` (propose: client work, containers for a client, cloud, past a budget or quota), the gate chosen by the server from the project's class, never by the manager. Runners: `local` (`claude --bg` in a worktree) first, then `container` (`claude -p --output-format stream-json --max-budget-usd`), `kaggle` (MetaSoar's `job.py`), `scheduled` (pg_cron); `cloud` not in v1. Workers report through one tool, `pm_report` (kind, a summary of at most 300 words, refs: branch, PR, commit, results), never a transcript. Workers are background sessions, never subagents: today's two agents stopped at the usage limit were subagents, which `claude agents --json` does not list (seen 2026-11-27, Claude Code 2.1.332). (d) **The supervisor**, on the host (`uv run`, its own login `pm_supervisor`), outside the manager's reach, applies the actuators and watches every runner, publishing `pm.worker.<state>` (working, needs_input, stalled, limited, finished, failed, stopped) and respawning a session stopped by the usage limit at its reset, one at a time. The trial `scripts/trials/manager/watch.py` is its watch half with no database; run once it read the one background session as `pm.worker.finished`. (e) **Decisions:** gated actions stay in the review lane (#32); questions and notices are `env.decision` rows the person accepts, edits, responds to or ignores; notifications only on needs-input, failed or stalled, a verdict, over budget. "Done" is a `judge` worker's verdict with a clean context (`morphogen change` or `judge`, or Morphogen's reviewer), never the worker's; the person merges. (f) **The UI** is a view module `desktop/view-manager/` (projects, workers with peek, log and stop, the inbox; spend from the gateway's meter), beside the Assistant window, where the manager now answers. (g) **First trial, PM-1**, registered here: nine messages in three rounds across two scratch repositories while workers run; falsified by more than one reply later than 30 s, any work attempt by the manager, more than one of six requests misrouted or not delegated, the manager's tokens over 10% of the workers', a worker's needs-input or finish reaching the inbox later than 60 s, or a worker lost. Build order and the owner's open questions (privacy of the manager's stream, whether it replaces the assistant, which projects are `own`, workers' defaults, cloud work, automatic resume, where the mirror lives) are in the document. | proposed |
